2026. 10.27
先週（10/19～10/26）発表の主な経済指標
米生産活動は堅調。AI関連投資を背景にコンピューター・電子
製品や機械などがけん引。一方、データセンター建設に伴う需要
増加が、他産業の生産や建設活動を圧迫する兆候も
コア消費者物価は再び2％超え。特殊要因を除くサービス価格
も加速し、人件費転嫁の兆し。連合は2027年春闘で6％中心
を要求。中小企業の余力に課題も、物価上振れに注意
注目点② サービス物価の加速で重み増す2027年春闘戦線注目点①AI投資の恩恵拡大、副作用もじわり顕在化
総合PMIは57.6と高水準を維持。新規失業保険申
請件数も19.4万件と低水準で解雇の動きは引き続
き限定的。一方で、住宅着工件数や住宅建設業
者の景況感は悪化、高金利の影響が継続

し、これを拒否

ベージュブック
（2026/7）
米国：AI関連を中心に製造業生産は堅調も、資材・設備の需給ひっ迫が他産業に波及
 米国の製造業生産は、AI関連投資を背景にコンピューター・電子製品、一次金属、一般機械などが堅調
― 一方、非耐久財や家具など消費関連の生産は軟調に推移し、業種間のばらつきが拡大
 FRBは、データセンター建設に伴う鋼材・電気設備などの需要増加が、他の建設案件や生産活動に影響していると指摘
―AI投資の拡大が関連産業の生産を押し上げる一方、供給制約を通じて他産業の活動を圧迫する兆候も
― 当面はAI投資による成長押し上げ効果が期待される一方、供給制約や価格上昇を通じた負の影響にも注意が必要
4

日本：全国コアCPIは再び2％超え。基調的なサービス価格も伸び高まる
5
 9月の全国コアCPIは前年比＋2.1％（前月：同＋1.7％）と加速。2025年12月以来の2％超に
― 原油高・円安等のコスト上昇もあり、家事用消耗品や教養娯楽用品等を中心に伸び率が拡大
― 一般サービス価格も伸び加速。シルバーウィークの並びによる宿泊料や外食費の上昇などが主因
 特殊要因を除いた一般サービス価格もじわり上昇
― 変動の大きい宿泊費や私立高校無償化による押し下げを除いた基調的な一般サービス価格に上昇圧力。人件費増の
価格転嫁が進んでいる可能性を示唆

全国コアCPI CPI一般サービス価格の推移

### Page 7

日本：連合は2027年春闘で賃上げ要求を強化。高水準の賃上げ持続と物価への波及が焦点
6
 連合は、2027年春闘賃上げ要求の目安を「6％中心、5％以上」と発表
―5％以上の賃上げを最低目標として明確化。実質賃金1％上昇の定着をめざす方針
― 中小企業は大企業との格差是正に向け「6％以上」が目標。過去4年のインフレで実質賃金が低下した分の回復も要求
― また、新たに、2030年に2026年の賃金水準を15％程度上回る中期目標を設定。持続的な賃金上昇をめざす
 賃上げ余力は企業規模・業種で濃淡。中小企業等で高い「賃上げ5％の壁」
― なお、6％に近い賃上げが実現する場合、サービス価格への波及が強まり、基調的物価上昇率が2％を上回る可能性
連合・春闘基本構想の新旧比較 春闘賃上げ率

基本構想」 より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

### Page 8

欧州：2027年度予算審議が本格化。生活苦によるデモが拡大する中、財政赤字は高水準
7
 2027年度予算審議が本格化。財政赤字は総じて高止まり
― 一定の防衛費・エネルギー関連支出を柔軟化する特例措置の適用後も、複数の国でEU財政基準で定める赤字幅を超
過。スペインは予算案を巡って議会解散。フランス・ベルギーも10/15時点でEUに予算案を未提出
 生活改善を求める抗議活動が欧州各国で発生。支出圧力の高まりは財政再建の障壁に
― フランスの学生によるデモを発端に、生活費の高騰や公共サービスの劣化に対する抗議活動は欧州各国に拡大。一部
は暴動に発展し、警察官と衝突する事態に
9～10月に発生した主な抗議活動主要国の財政収支実績と見通し
（出所） 各種報道より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

欧州：金融市場は政治・財政を巡る混乱を嫌気。不透明感は2027年入り後も継続する見込み
8
 金融市場は政治・財政を巡る混乱を警戒。主要国の選挙を控える中、不透明感は2027年まで継続へ
― 抗議活動が広がるイタリア、フランス、ベルギーの一般政府債務残高（GDP比）は、EU加盟国の中でも上位（それぞれ2
位、3位、4位）。金利上昇の背景は政治・財政への懸念
― 影響は為替市場にも波及。9月中旬以降、ユーロはドルに対して弱含み

26年1月 株価指数算出会社が株式保有構造の不透明さを問題視し、市場区分引き下げの可能性を警告

26年6月

の確認が必要と指摘。改善が不十分なら格下げを検討する
と警告

## Document 073: 2026年9月雇用統計

Source PDF: all report pdf/2026年9月雇用統計.pdf
Report source: DIR
Date: 2026-10-30
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

日本

株式会社大和総研 丸の内オフィス 〒100-6756 東京都千代田区丸の内一丁目 9番 1号 グラントウキョウ ノースタワー 
このレポートは投資勧誘を意図して提供するものではありません。このレポートの掲載情報は信頼できると考えられる情報源から作成しておりますが、その正確性、完全
性を保証するものではありません。また、記載された意見や予測等は作成時点のものであり今後予告なく変更されることがあります。㈱大和総研と大和証券㈱は、㈱大和
証券グループ本社を親会社とする大和証券グループの会社です。内容に関する一切の権利は㈱大和総研にあります。無断での複製・転載・転送等はご遠慮ください。

2026年 10月 30日 全8頁

Indicators Update 
2026年 9月雇用統計 
失業率は2.4％と前月から0.1％pt低下し、就業拡大が進展

経済調査部 エコノミスト 菊池 慈陽

[要約] 
◼ 2026年 9月の完全失業率（季節調整値）は 2.4％と、2カ月ぶりに低下（前月差▲
0.1％pt）し、7月の水準に戻った。失業者数は「自発的な離職」を中心に 2カ月ぶり
に減少（同▲10万人）し、就業者数は2カ月連続で増加（同＋34万人）した。労働参
加と就業拡大が進展し、雇用環境は前月から改善したとみられる。 
◼ 2026年9月の有効求人倍率（季節調整値）は1.19倍（前月差＋0.01pt）と3カ月ぶり
に上昇した。新規求職申込件数が大きく減少したことで、新規求人倍率は2.19倍（同
＋0.13pt）と上昇した。 
◼ 先行きの雇用環境は堅調に推移しよう。労働供給が中長期的に減少していく可能性が
高いこともあり、企業は高水準の賃上げなど、人材確保に向けた積極的な取り組みを
続けている。ただし、雇用環境の悪化リスクは小さくない。中東情勢をめぐる不確実
性は依然として高く、長期化する原油高が企業収益を悪化させ、幅広い産業で労働需
要を下押しする恐れがある。

9月の完全失業率：2.4％と前月から低下、「自発的な離職」による失業者が大きく減少 
2026年 9月の完全失業率（季節調整値）は2.4％と、2カ月ぶりに低下（前月差▲0.1％pt）
し、7月の水準に戻った（図表2左上） 。失業者数は2カ月ぶりに減少（同▲10万人）し、就業
者数は 2カ月連続で増加（同＋34万人）した。労働参加と就業拡大が進展し、雇用環境は前月
から改善したとみられる。 
失業者数を求職理由別に見ると、「自発的な離職」 （前月差▲13万人）が大きく減少し、全体
を押し下げた。「勤め先や事業の都合による離職」（同▲1万人）は小幅に減少した。他方、「定
年又は雇用契約の満了」（同＋1万人）と「新たに求職」（同＋1万人）は小幅に増加した（巻末
の雇用概況①下段左）。 
就業者数の前月からの変化を業種別に見ると、「製造業」と「医療，福祉」が大きく増加し、
全体を押し上げた。「卸売業，小売業」と「情報通信業」も増加した。他方、「建設業」やその
他の非製造業は減少した。（図表2左下）。 
雇用者数（役員を除く）を雇用形態別に見ると、正規雇用者は前月から 22万人増加した。
2026年春頃から増加傾向にあり、ピークだった2025年 11月の水準を上回った。非正規雇用者
は 5カ月ぶりに増加（前月差＋21万人）した（図表 2右下）。

9月の新規求人倍率：求職が大きく減少した一方で求人は増加し、前月から上昇 
2026年 9月の有効求人倍率（季節調整値）は 1.19倍（前月差＋0.01pt）と 3カ月ぶりに上
昇した。新規求人倍率は2.19倍（同＋0.13pt）と上昇した（図表3左）。 
求人側の動きを見ると、有効求人数は前月から横ばいだった一方、新規求人数（前月比＋
1.5％）は 2カ月ぶりに増加した（図表 4左） 。求職側では、有効求職者数（同▲1.4％）、新規
求職申込件数（同▲4.4％）はいずれも 3カ月ぶりに減少した。求職側は 2025年春頃から持ち
直しの動きが見られており、求人側も2026年春頃から増加傾向にある。 
雇用形態別に見ると、正社員の有効求人倍率（季節調整値）は1.01倍（前月差＋0.01pt）と
3カ月ぶりに上昇した。新規求人倍率も 1.79倍（同＋0.11pt）と 3カ月ぶりに上昇した（図表
3右）。

先行き：堅調な推移を見込むも、中東情勢など雇用環境の悪化リスクは小さくない 
先行きの雇用環境は、堅調に推移することを見込んでいる。労働供給が中長期的に減少して
いく可能性が高いこともあり、企業は高水準の賃上げなど1、人材確保に向けた積極的な取り組
みを続けている。 
日本銀行「全国企業短期経済観測調査」 （日銀短観）の2026年 9月調査では、雇用人員判断
DI（全規模全産業、 「過剰」－「不足」 ）は「最近」が▲38％ptと 6月調査から 1％pt低下し
（人手不足感が強まり）、「先行き」は▲41％ptと更なる低下が予想されている（巻末の雇用概
況①上段右）。特に、中小企業（全産業）は「最近」が▲42％ptと6月調査から3％pt低下し、
「先行き」は▲45％ptだった。中小企業を中心に、人手不足感が強まっているとみられる。 
株式会社ナウキャストによる「HRog賃金 Now」の求人数指数を見ると、正社員とパート・ア
ルバイトのいずれも 2026年春頃から上昇傾向にある（巻末の雇用概況③下段左）。人手不足感
の強まりを受けて、今後も労働需要は堅調に推移するとみられる。 
他方、トランプ米政権による高関税政策（トランプ関税）1F
2や、長期化する日中関係の冷え込
みは、引き続き労働需要の下押し要因となるだろう。 
また、雇用環境の悪化リスクは小さくない。中東情勢は依然として不確実性が高い状況が続
いており、とりわけ注意が必要だ。原油等の供給網の正常化は当面見込みにくく、輸入原油価
格が高止まりしている。原油高は原材料費や物流費などの上昇を通じて企業収益を悪化させ3、
幅広い産業で労働需要を下押しする可能性がある。

1 日本労働組合総連合会（連合）が公表した第 7回（最終）回答集計結果では、定期昇給相当込みの賃上げ率
（加重平均）が企業規模計で 5.01％と、前年（5.25％）をやや下回るものの、高水準を維持している。300人
未満の中小組合では 4.69％と前年（4.65％）をわずかながら上回り、中小企業の積極的な姿勢が目立った。詳
細は、日本労働組合総連合会（連合）「3年連続の 5％超え！～2026春季生活闘争 第7回（最終）回答集計結
果について～」（2026年7月3日）を参照。 
2 トランプ米政権は通商法 122条に基づく措置が失効する 7月 24日に新たな関税措置を発動した（日本経済新
聞 電子版「トランプ政権が新関税発動、日本 12.5％ 中間選挙へ看板政策継続」（2026年7月 24日））。 
3 原油高による物価の上昇については、神田慶司・畑中宏仁・中村華奈子・横田凱「日本経済見通し：2026年
8月」（大和総研レポート、2026年8月 24日）を参照。

## Document 140: 261031_nmr_MTK

Source PDF: all report pdf/261031_nmr_MTK.pdf

Date: 2026-10-31

### Page 1

EQUITY: FABLESS
MediaTek   2454.TW 2454 TT
Global Markets Research
30 October 2026
Dispels recent market noises with upside surprise; retain Buy
Quick Note
Maintain Buy and TP of TWD5,800
We recently noted that MediaTek’s earnings call could prove to be a positive catalyst for 
its share price, considering a number of unfounded concerns and noises had taken center 
stage before the call (Preview: market concerns overdone ). However, we believe some of 
MTK’s messages during earnings call turned out to be even more bullish than we had 
expected, including: (1) TPU v.8 contribution of USD2.5bn in 4Q26E (vs. previous 
guidance of more than USD2bn , and Street concerns around MTK potentially missing the 
USD2bn target); (2) management now expects ASIC sales in 2027E to reach the high end 
(USD16bn) of its prior guidance range (USD14-16bn) vs. our forecast of USD14bn; (3) 
TPU v.9 schedule is on track for 1Q28E mass production (a powerful clarification vs. 
Street expectation of 1-2 quarters’ delay); (4) management is very confident about v.9 
EMIB-T future yield trend after resolving 2-3 key issues (implying no CoWoS back-up plan, 
vs. Street expectation of a CoWoS-based v.9 project being opened); (5) Mediatek 
announced that it has secured a 2nd ASIC customer with sales contribution from 2028E; 
(6) ASIC-driven operating leverage will kick in from 4Q26E (guidance suggesting 19% 
OPM in 4Q26E, vs our estimate of 18.6% and 3Q26’s 16%; and management expects 
2027E OPM to be above 20%, vs our estimate of 22%). The only downside surprise might 
be its 4Q26E sales guidance, which suggests 25%-32% q-q growth vs. our estimate of 
34% q-q. We would watch if this is a low bar for MediaTek to beat. GPM guidance of 
45%+-1.5% is 1pp lower than previous quarter’s range (46%+-1.5%; and 46.3% actual in 
3Q26) due to ASIC — in line with our forecast of 45.4%. Overall, we expect the share 
price to react positively to the earnings call and, thus, reiterate our Buy rating and 
TWD5,800 TP, based on 25x unchanged 2027-28 EPS estimate. The stock is currently 
trading at 16.9x.

Fig. 1: MediaTek's 3Q26 results and 4Q26F guidance vs Nomura forecasts and Bloomberg consensus

### Page 2

MediaTek: 3Q26 analysts briefing summary
Below comments are from MediaTek’s management, unless otherwise stated :
Results summary
3Q26 revenue was TWD168.1bn (+10.4% q-q, +18.3% y-y), above the high end of
the TWD152.2–159.8bn guidance.
The beat came mainly from better-than-expected customer demand and
improving supply.
○
Growth was driven by volume production of several new Smart Edge projects and
higher DRAM content in TV SoC.
○
•
GM was 46.3% (+0.1pp q-q, -0.2pp y-y), within the 46% ±1.5pp guidance.•

Opex ratio was 30.2% (vs 31.2% in 2Q26 and 30.9% in 3Q25), within the 31% ±2pp
guidance.
•
OPM was 16.1% (+1.1pp q-q, +0.5pp y-y).•
Net income was TWD28.2bn (+14.6% q-q, +10.8% y-y). Net margin was 16.8%
(+0.6pp q-q, -1.1pp y-y).
•
EPS was TWD17.50 (TWD15.28 in 2Q26, TWD15.84 in 3Q25). Non-TIFRS EPS was
TWD17.92.
•
Average FX was TWD31.99/USD (2Q26: 31.60; guidance assumption: 32).•
Prepared remarks
MediaTek completed a USD3.9bn overseas convertible bond offering in 3Q, with

participation from nVidia (NVDA US, Not rated) and Alphabet (GOOGL US, Not
rated).
This strengthens partnerships with key AI infrastructure players and helps secure
capacity for critical components.
○
•
MediaTek continues to make solid progress in both data center and edge devices.
Datacenter ASIC
Current gen: Demand remains very strong. Initial shipments began in
September, and MediaTek is confident of delivering USD2.5bn of revenue in
4Q26.
The 4Q upside comes from robust customer demand. MediaTek has

worked closely with supply chain partners to secure supply, including
substrates.
■
For 2027E, the program is expected to reach the high end of the revenue
target range, about USD16bn, or around 20% of the AI accelerator ASIC
market. This is underpinned by a solid supply outlook, and MediaTek is
working to secure additional capacity to meet demand.
■
•
Next gen: Development is progressing well and is on track for HVM in 1Q28.
The chip is purpose-built for the customer’s next-generation AI

infrastructure. It delivers multiple times higher performance per watt at a
significantly lower TCO.
■
It is expected to strengthen MediaTek’s market position and contribute
substantially to revenue growth.
■
•
Technology: Solid progress continues in 448G SerDes, 3.5D advanced
packaging, and CPO.
•
Broader customer base: MediaTek is expanding beyond silicon into system
and rack solutions. It connects custom xPUs to the NVLink Fusion ecosystem
and also supports UALink.

•
MediaTek continues to expand engagements with several customers.•
○
Edge
The RTX Spark AI PC (N1X), which integrates an nVidia GPU, became
commercially available in mid-October through major PC OEMs. Next-gen
SoC development is already underway.
•
Dimensity 9500 powers the latest Samsung Galaxy AI tablets. This is the third
consecutive generation of Samsung AI tablets using a MediaTek flagship
SoC.
•
○
•

Mobile: -1% q-q, -17% y-y (37% of revenue).
Flagship outperformed as customers launched new AI flagship smartphones,
including models with MediaTek’s first 2nm SoC, the Dimensity 9600.
○
•
Smart Edge: +22% q-q, +63% y-y (58% of revenue).
Y-y growth was driven by volume production of new projects: a 5G thin modem for
a new smartphone customer, new AI tablets, AI PCs, and automotive cockpits. TV

SoC revenue rose on higher DRAM content.
○
•
Power IC: +1% q-q, +18% y-y (5% of revenue).
Y-y growth came mainly from share gains in data-center-related business.○

4Q26 guidance
Revenue is guided at TWD210.1–221.8bn (+25–32% q-q, +40–48% y-y), driven by
the AI accelerator ramp-up, despite seasonal softness in consumer electronics.
•
GM is guided at 45% ±1.5pp.
Datacenter business blended GM is still below the corporate average, but OPM
becomes accretive from 4Q.

○
Price adjustments for non-DC businesses reflect supply chain cost increases.○
•
Opex ratio is guided at 26% ±2pp.
4Q marks the starting point of OPM improvement. MediaTek expects 2027E OPM
of 20%+.
○
•
FX assumption is TWD31.7/USD.•
Full-year revenue growth is now expected to exceed the previous target of high-
single-digit growth in USD.
•
Q&A
Any changes in ASIC schedule and milestones?
The 2nd ASIC is on track and on schedule.
MediaTek is aware of market noise about the tape-out schedule. The chip has

multiple dies, and a couple have already taped out.
○
The large, more complex die will also tape out on schedule, though it takes about
three weeks longer than the others.
○
•
2028 will be another year of strong y-y growth based on the demand profile. No
details yet, as the supply chain is still being finalized.
•
EMIB-T confidence
MediaTek is working with the US IDM’s packaging process and has built a strong in-
house manufacturing team led by experienced fab veterans.

•
It is working closely with the IDM and substrate suppliers.•
Management has high confidence that yield will improve quickly once two or three key
problems are resolved.
MediaTek is working with both Taiwanese and Japanese suppliers.○
•
MediaTek is not dependent on a single supplier.
TSMC (2330 TT, Buy) remains its most important partner, and the two work
closely across the entire technology chain.
○
•
Next-next gen, TAM and contribution
MediaTek is working very closely with the customer on the next-next generation.•

The customer is aggressive in pushing performance up and TCO down.•
This will be another challenging chip, and the definition will take time to finalize.•
Management will share more when the time is right.•
Nomura | MediaTek 30 October 2026

### Page 4

New ASIC client
A 2nd customer is confirmed, separate from the lead customer.•
The project has kicked off, with first revenue and mass production expected in 2028.•
The customer is not from China.•
Vendor financing for LLM customers?
MediaTek will consider it if necessary and if it is the right customer.•
Returns versus capex have improved over the past six quarters. Management 
believes capex, whether from CSPs or frontier model companies, will generate 
enough revenue and cash flow. Some support may be needed during the transition 
period before cash generation stabilizes.
•
EMIB-T / HBM5 / technology capability and competition
The entry barrier is the ability to integrate multiple technologies into manufacturable, 
scalable products: advanced packaging, HBM5, 448G SerDes, and low power. It is 
not any single technology.
•
Competitors are strong, and MediaTek does not assume it is far ahead. However, it is 
confident it will win its share of the business.
•
NVLink Fusion benefits and customer interest
It is a business model with strong technical content, and it makes it much easier for 
customers to integrate their own designs.
•
The key advantage is much faster time to compute power.•
MediaTek and nVidia are in discussions with several potential customers. Feedback 
is positive, and MediaTek hopes to announce cases in the near term.
•
448G
448G SerDes has already taped out.•
448G is a must going forward. It is an N6 design with very stringent power 
consumption requirements.
•
It will not be on the critical path, and MediaTek will improve interoperability to serve a 
broader customer base.
•

Nomura | MediaTek 30 October 2026

Definition of Nomura Group's equity research rating system and sectors
The rating system is a relative system, indicating expected performance against a specific benchmark identified for each individual stock, 
subject to limited management discretion. An analyst’s target price is an assessment of the current intrinsic fair value of the stock based on an 
appropriate valuation methodology determined by the analyst. Valuation methodologies include, but are not limited to, discounted cash flow 
analysis, expected return on equity and multiple analysis. Analysts may also indicate expected absolute upside/downside relative to the stated 
target price, defined as (target price - current price)/current price.

SECTORS
A 'Bullish' stance, indicates that the analyst expects the sector to outperform the Benchmark during the next 12 months. A 'Neutral' stance, 
indicates that the analyst expects the sector to perform in line with the Benchmark during the next 12 months. A 'Bearish' stance, indicates that 
the analyst expects the sector to underperform the Benchmark during the next 12 months. Sectors that are labelled as 'Not rated' or shown as 
'N/A' are not assigned ratings. Benchmarks are as follows: United States: S&P 500; Europe: Dow Jones STOXX 600; Global Emerging 
Markets (ex-Asia): MSCI Emerging Markets ex-Asia. Japan/Asia ex-Japan: Sector ratings are not assigned.

Target Price
A Target Price, if discussed, indicates the analyst’s forecast for the share price with a 12-month time horizon, reflecting in part the analyst's 
estimates for the company's earnings. The achievement of any target price may be impeded by general market and macroeconomic trends, and 
by other risks related to the company or the market, and may not occur if the company's earnings differ from estimates.

## Document 229: [012450] 한화에어로스페이스(012450) 3Q26 Review_ 악재의 중첩, 4분기까지 지켜보자한화에어로스페이스(012450) 3Q26 Review_ 악재의 중첩, 4분기까_b6b79840

Source PDF: all report pdf/[012450] 한화에어로스페이스(012450) 3Q26 Review_ 악재의 중첩, 4분기까지 지켜보자한화에어로스페이스(012450) 3Q26 Review_ 악재의 중첩, 4분기까_b6b79840.pdf

3Q26 Review: 악재의 중첩,

최정환 CFA

jeonghwan.choi@daishin.com

투자의견 BUY  
매수, 유지 
6개월 목표주가 1,640,000 
유지  
현재주가 
(26.10.29) 
739,000 
방위산업

◼ 투자의견 매수, 목표주가 1,640,000원 유지 
◼ 컨센서스 및 추정치 대비 매출, 영업이익 하회 
◼ 4Q26 및 27년 실적 추정치 상향

투자의견 매수, 목표주가 1,640,000원 유지 
4Q26E-2Q27E 지상방산 및 항공우주 EBITDA에 Target 12MF EV/EBITDA 25.0x 
적용, 상장 자회사 가치 SOTP 통해 목표주가 산출. 3Q26 실적 아쉬우나 투자포
인트에는 변함 없어 멀티플은 변경 없이 적용. 27년 실적 추정치 상향에 따라 본
업 가치 상승, 26년 스페인 자주포 양산 및 미국 자주포 시제 사업자로 선정되며 
현지화 전략이 유효했음을 증명. 3Q26 실적에 대한 우려를 소화하며 글로벌 피어
들과의 멀티플 격차 축소를 전망함

컨센서스 및 추정치 대비 매출, 영업이익 하회 
3Q26 연결 기준 매출액 6.1조원(-6.0% YoY), 영업이익 4,520억원(-46.2% YoY, 
OPM 7.4%)으로 컨센서스인 매출액 7.4조원 및 영업이익 1.1조원을 크게 하회
하는 실적 시현. 하회의 주요 요인으로는 3Q26 필리조선소 적자폭 확대, 지상
방산 부문 실적이 부진한 영향. 지상방산 부문의 경우 매출액 1.2조원(-41.0% 
YoY), 영업이익 220억원(-96.2% YoY, OPM 1.7%)을 시현했는데, 폴란드 관련 
매출이 부재했던 1Q26 영업이익률 16.9%보다도 낮은 실적을 보임. 이는 국내
외 사업 모두에서 악재가 겹쳐 발생했던 것으로 보이는데 국내 사업에서는 마
진율이 낮은 사업이 매출로 인식되었고 수출 사업에서는 폴란드 매출 부재 뿐 
아니라 이집트, 호주 K9 자주포 매출 인식 또한 예상 대비 부진해 고정비 레버
리지 효과로 3Q26 영업이익률이 크게 훼손된 것으로 추정. 이외 대전사업장 복
구에 따른 비용, 기타 사업 충당금 등 총 500억원 수준의 일회성 비용이 발생했
으며 영업외손익에서는 원화 강세에 따른 환손실이 주로 반영되었음

4Q26 및 27년 실적 추정치 상향 
1Q26에서 3Q26까지 이뤄지지 않았던 폴란드 EC2 K9 자주포 30문 인도는 
4Q26에 집중될 것으로 보이며 폴란드 EC1 천무 또한 기존 가이던스였던 26년 
천무 40대에서 60대로 인도 대수 상향이 이뤄짐. 이에 따라 4Q26 지상방산 영
업이익률은 22.8%로 회복을 전망. 27년에는 폴란드 K9 자주포 및 천무 인도 대
수 증가로 26년 수출 비중 58.0%에서 77.2%로 증가, 이에 따라 영업이익률 또
한 매출 믹스 개선에 따라 19.8%에서 28.0%로 개선을 전망

- Buy(매수)  
  : 향후 6개월간 시장수익률 대비 10%p 이상 주가 상승 예상 
- Marketperform(시장수익률) 
  : 향후 6개월간 시장수익률 대비 -10%p~10%p 주가 변동 예상 
- Underperform(시장수익률 하회) 
  : 향후 6개월간 시장수익률 대비 10%p 이상 주가 하락 예상

## Document 254: [373220] LG에너지솔루션(373220) Long Trading 구간LG에너지솔루션(373220) Long Trading 구간Long Trading 구간 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[373220] LG에너지솔루션(373220) Long Trading 구간LG에너지솔루션(373220) Long Trading 구간Long Trading 구간 - 기업.pdf

### Page 1

www.imeritz.com

LG에너지솔루션 (373220)

Company Brief 
이차전지/석유화학 
Analyst 노우호 
wooho.rho@meritz.co.kr 
RA 황현식 
hyeonsig.hwang@meritz.co.kr Long Trading 구간  
3Q26P 잠정실적 발표 이후: ESS ↑, BEV ↓  
LG에너지솔루션의 3Q26P 잠정실적 발표 이후 배터리 섹터는 11/3 미국 중간선거
를 전후로 Trading Long 투자의견이 유효하다. 미국 중간선거 이후 특정정당 우세와 
별개로, 미국 Ex-China ESS 공급망 프리미엄은 유효하다. 동사 역시 그간의 ESS 
사업 난제(단위당 수율, 팩 병목)는 점진적 해소될 점이다. 반면 시장의 BEV 반등 기
대감에 동의하지 않는다. EU 프랑스와 독일의 내연기관 퇴출시점 지연 움직임 및 미
국 연비규제 정책 폐지 결정으로 전통 완성차 고객사들향 BEV 배터리 출하 부진은 
여전하겠다. 미국의 BEV 정책 변화시점은 28.11월 대선으로 지연될 전망이다.

ESS 모멘텀: 한국 제3차 ESS 중앙계약시장 사업자 선정 모멘텀 
대다수 시장 참여자들은 2026년 연말까지 LG에너지솔루션의 미국 ESS 60Gwh  규
모의 잔여 수주 달성 가시성에 집중한다. 해당 ESS 사업 수주 가이던스 관건은 시기
보다 방향성이다. 단, 당사가 주목하는 연말 모멘텀은 한국 정부향 제3차 ESS 중앙
계약시장 사업자 선정으로 판단한다. 3차 규모는 1.13Gw로 1&2차 계약 규모(합산 
1.13Gw) 대비 확대되었다. 또한 언론보도상 LG에너지솔루션은 해당 수주를 위한 핵
심 4대 소재 등 전 Value-chain 국산화를 선언한 바 있다. 해당 수주 확정시, 동사
를 비롯한 해당 Value-chain에 긍정적 모멘텀이 예상된다.

투자의견 Buy, 적정주가 51만원 제시   
26년 연중 지정학 Risk, 에너지 대전환 영향 등에 전력 인프라 수요는 꾸준할 전망
이다. 향후 국내 이차전지 산업은 ‘L’ Shaped Cycle 전망, 반면 동사는 스마트카 T사
향 원통형 전지 출하량 증가, ESS 제조업 이상으로 변모하는 과정으로 산업 평균 성
장률 Outperform 가능하다. 투자의견 Buy 유지, 적정주가는 추정치 상향에 51만원
을 제시한다.

Meritz Research  2026. 10. 15

## Document 310: Consumer Checkpoint_ Strength in numbers September saw solid spending across many categories and a strong start to the holidays. 2 min read

Source PDF: all report pdf/Consumer Checkpoint_ Strength in numbers September saw solid spending across many categories and a strong start to the holidays. 2 min read.pdf
Report source: Bank of America
Date: 2026-10-01
Pages: 9
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

13026023     1

Economy 
Consumer Checkpoint: Strength in numbers 
09 October 2026

Key takeaways 
• Consumer spending remained very solid in September. Bank of America aggregated credit and debit card spending per 
household rose 6.2% year-over-year (YoY), or 5.1% excluding gasoline, both the third strongest growth rates over the past four 
years. Month-over-month (MoM) spending was more muted, especially without gas. 
• Spending shows considerable breadth: the vast majority of discretionary retail and services categories are showing growth. 
Moreover, this strength extends across income cohorts, with the ongoing recovery of lower- and middle-income households' 
spending growth. 
• With the winter months fast approaching, holiday spending is already increasing at a solid rate, though it's still early. In Bank of 
America deposit data through September, buy now, pay later (BNPL) usage is rising but not at a rate that is overly concerning. 
However, nearly one in every five households that use BNPL has a repayment of over $500.

Consumer Checkpoint is a regular publication from Bank of America Institute. It aims to provide a holistic and real-time estimate of US 
consumers’ spending and their financial well-being, leveraging the depth and breadth of Bank of America proprietary data. Such data is not 
intended to be reflective or indicative of, and should not be relied upon as, the results of operations, financial conditions or performance of 
Bank of America. 
A solid September for spending   
Consumer spending continued to look solid in September. Bank of America total aggregated credit and debit card spending per 
household rose 6.2% year-over-year (YoY), while spending excluding gasoline increased 5.1% YoY. Notably, both are the third 
strongest growth rates in the past four years. Seasonally-adjusted total card spending rose 0.2% month-over-month (MoM), 
while spending excluding gasoline was little changed. Overall, spending growth remains well above the 2025 average rate 
(Exhibit 1). 
Exhibit 1: Total card spending per household rose 6.2% YoY and 5.1% YoY excluding gasoline in September 
Total card spending growth per household, based on Bank of America aggregated credit and debit card data (monthly, MoM%, seasonally adjusted (SA)) and 
(monthly, YoY%, non-SA, right-hand side (rhs))

Source: Bank of America internal data

Most spending categories are growing  
One striking thing about recent card spending, in our view, is that it is well balanced across categories. For example, both 
spending on retail (excluding gasoline) and services grew strongly YoY in September, at 3.5% and 5.4%, respectively. Meanwhile, 
retail spending is showing notably better momentum than in 2025 (Exhibit 2).  
Exhibit 2: Spending is rising across retail and services 
Retail (excluding gasoline) and services card spending per household (monthly, seasonally adjusted, index January 2025 = 100)

Source: Bank of America internal data 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Well balanced across “nice-to-have” and “need-to-have” retail categories  
Is the momentum in retail spending reflective of “nice-to-have” discretionary or “need-to-have” necessity spending? In retail, 
this is a nuanced question, as many categories sit in a gray area between “necessity” and “discretionary”. General merchandise 
stores, for example, sell both essentials like groceries as well as nice-to-have electronics or even jewelry.  
In Exhibit 3, we account for this by grouping retail spending into “more discretionary” (furniture, electronics, building materials, 
online retailers, clothing retailers and miscellaneous stores), “partly discretionary” (auto, health and personal care retailers, 
general merchandise) and “mostly necessity” (gas and groceries). Even within these categories the edges will be blurred, but the 
overall impression is that retail spending growth is widely distributed, with spending rising across all fronts. One caveat: “mostly 
discretionary” spending has been making a smaller contribution since June. 
Exhibit 3: Retail spending is distributed across discretionary and necessity categories  
Percentage points contribution to YoY growth by type of retail spending (three-month moving average)

Source: Bank of America internal data. See methodology for category aggregation definitions.  
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Looking at the share of “more discretionary” retail spending categories that are increasing YoY underscores that growth is well 
balanced and broad-based (Exhibit 4). Non-store and online retailers and miscellaneous stores saw some of the fastest growth, 
while furniture and building materials were among the laggards, though other durable categories showed some strength.

While some of this spending growth likely reflects the impact of higher prices pushing up spending across a swathe of

For services, growth is driven mostly by discretionary spending  
In services spending, on the other hand, the boundaries between discretionary and necessity spending are often clearer. Travel 
spending (e.g., airlines, lodging) can be thought of as, by and large, discretionary. At the same time, utility bills, rent and 
insurance payments are necessities.  
And in services, discretionary spending remains the clear driver, accounting for over three-quarters of YoY spending growth in 
September (Exhibit 6). Leisure and housing services are major components of growth, though travel and restaurants also made 
positive contributions. However, as we noted last month strength in airline spending largely reflects price rises rather than 
transactions growth (read more in Consumer Checkpoint: Still sizzling).  
Exhibit 6: Services spending growth has been mostly driven by discretionary spending over the past year 
Percentage points contribution to growth by type of services spending (three-month moving average)

Source: Bank of America internal data

Lower-income households’ spending outpaced all but the top 5% 
The breadth in spending growth across categories is also evident across income cohorts. In September, lower-income 
households’ total card spending growth was 5.5% YoY, while the equivalent figures for middle- and higher-income households 
were 4.9% and 4.8% YoY, respectively. The top 5% of households by income continue to show the fastest spending growth, 
5.9% in September, though this has eased somewhat (Exhibit 7). 
Overall, the divergences across income cohorts are much smaller than they were in 2025 and the first three months of this year. 
Just focusing on spending excluding key necessities (gasoline, food and utilities) doesn’t meaningfully change the picture of 
convergence across income cohorts (Exhibit 8).  
Exhibit 7: The top 5% saw the strongest spending growth of any 
income cohort… 
Total card spending per household by income tercile and the top 5% 
(three-month moving average, YoY%, SA)

Source: Bank of America internal data 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

Exhibit 8: …though discretionary spending for the top 5% has eased 
some since June 
Discretionary card spending per household by income tercile and the top 
5% (three-month moving average, YoY%, SA)

Source: Bank of America internal data 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

The strength in lower- and middle-income households’ discretionary spending is occurring despite necessity spending shifting 
from a tailwind to a headwind over the past year amid rising gas prices (Exhibit 9). In our view, a key component of why these 
households have been able to combine rising necessity spending with strength in discretionary is the labor market and a 
recovery in their after-tax wage growth (Exhibit 10).  
In September, lower-income wage growth eased to 4.5% YoY from 4.7% YoY in August, while growth for middle- and higher-
income households rose to 3.8% and 3.7% YoY, respectively. These changes were modest, and lower-income households are 
continuing to see faster after-tax wage growth than other cohorts, a notable turnaround from the “K-shaped” pattern we 
observed over much of 2025 and 2026.  
Exhibit 9: Necessity spending has gone from a tailwind to a 
headwind across all income cohorts

a narrowing gap

Should we be concerned about the easing in household spending and after-tax wage growth for those with the top 5% of 
incomes? For now, probably not, as in our view this spending growth is likely being driven by wealth effects rather than wage 
gains. Absent a sharp correction in equity markets, there is little reason to expect them to retrench their spending. However, 
developments here are worth watching as this cohort comprises a significant proportion of overall consumer spending. They also 
tend to make outsized contributions to several discretionary spending categories (Exhibit 11).  
Exhibit 11: The top 5% of households by income make up a disproportionate amount of spending in highly discretionary categories 
Share of spending by the top 5% of households by income across select discretionary categories (three-month moving average to September 2026, %)

Source: Bank of America internal data 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
With summer gone, thoughts turn to the holidays and BNPL… 
Now that summer is over, the next big gauge of consumer momentum is how people spend during the run-up to, and over, the 
holiday season. It is early days, but September spending on holiday items ran well ahead of 2025 (Exhibit 12). Given the backdrop 
of firmer after-tax wage growth and breadth across retail and services categories, this is perhaps not surprising. However, some 
of this spending strength could be due to rising prices as opposed to more purchases.  
Exhibit 12: It’s early days, but holiday spending is strong so far this year

A concern often raised as we move into the holidays is whether consumers will lean on less traditional forms of credit such as 
buy now, pay later (BNPL) to finance their spending. Frequent BNPL users tend to have higher credit card utilization rates (read 
more in: Do consumers have wiggle room to absorb higher gas prices?).  
Looking at the share of households in Bank of America deposit data with a BNPL payment, we do tend to see a seasonal rise 
over the holidays (Exhibit 13), though in past years, these increases have quickly reversed. Further, while the underlying trend in 
BNPL usage is upwards, there are no signs of a sharp acceleration. We do, however, see some evidence that BNPL is being used 
to finance more expensive purchases, with the share of households making repayments above $500 rising, albeit relatively slowly 
(Exhibit 14).  
Exhibit 13: 8.3% of Bank of America households had a BNPL 
payment in September

repayment of over $500

(monthly, $ amounts)

Source: Bank of America internal data 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

The tendency towards greater usage is occurring across income cohorts, though is somewhat more pronounced for lower-
income households (Exhibit 15). However, higher-income households have the largest share of BNPL payments above $500 
(Exhibit 16). Overall, in our view, the increase in the share of BNPL users heading into the season does not appear too 
concerning, though the rate of usage for some BNPL consumers does bear watching.

Methodology  
Selected Bank of America transaction data is used to inform the macroeconomic views expressed in this report and should be considered in the 
context of other economic indicators and publicly available information. In certain instances, the data may provide directional and/or predictive 
value. The data used is not comprehensive; it is based on aggregated and anonymized selections of Bank of America data and may reflect a 
degree of selection bias and limitations on the data available. 
Any payments data represents aggregated spend from US Retail, Preferred, Small Business and Wealth Management clients with a deposit 
account or credit card. Aggregated spend include total credit card, debit card, ACH, wires, bill pay, business/peer-to-peer, cash, and checks.  
Any Small Business payments data represents aggregate spend from Small Business clients with a deposit account or a Small Business credit 
card. Payroll payments data include channels such as ACH (automated clearing house), bill pay, checks and wire. Bank of America per Small 
Business client data represents activity spending from active Small Business clients with a deposit account or a Small Business credit card and 
at least one transaction in each month. Small businesses in this report include business clients within Bank of America and generally defined as 
under $5mm in annual sales revenue.  
Unless otherwise stated, data is not adjusted for seasonality, processing days or portfolio changes, and may be subject to periodic revisions.  
The differences between the total and per household card spending growth rate (if discussed) can be explained by the following reasons:  
1. Overall total card spending growth is partially boosted by the growth in the number of active cardholders in our sample. This could be due to 
an increasing customer base or inactive customers using their cards more frequently. 
2. Per household card spending growth only looks at households that complete at least five transactions with Bank of America cards in the 
month. Per household spending growth isolates impacts from a changing sample size, which could be unrelated to underlying economic 
momentum, and potential spending volatility from less active users. 
3. Overall total card spending includes small business card spending while per household card spending does not. 
4. Differences due to using processing dates (total card spending) versus transaction date (per household card spending). 
5. Other differences including household formations due to young adults moving in and out of their parent’s houses during COVID. 
Any household consumer deposit data based on Bank of America internal data is derived by anonymizing and aggregating data from Bank of 
America consumer deposit accounts in the US and analyzing that data at a highly aggregated level. Whenever median household savings and 
checking balances are quoted, the data is based on a fixed cohort of households that had a consumer deposit account (checking and/or savings 
account) for all months from January 2019 through the most current month of data shown.  
Bank of America aggregated credit/debit card spending per household includes spending from active US households only. Only consumer card 
holders making a minimum of five transactions a month are included in the dataset. Spending from corporate cards are excluded. Data 
regarding merchants who receive payments are identified and classified by the Merchant Categorization Code (MCC) defined by financial 
services companies. The data are mapped using proprietary methods from the MCCs to the North American Industry Classification System 
(NAICS), which is also used by the Census Bureau, in order to classify spending data by subsector. Spending data may also be classified by other 
proprietary methods not using MCCs.  
Store category aggregation for “more discretionary”, “partly discretionary”, and “mostly necessity” is defined as follows: 
More discretionary: furniture, electronics, building materials, hobbies, miscellaneous stores, nonstore retail, clothing. 
Partly discretionary: auto, health and personal care, general merchandise. 
Mostly necessity: Gasoline, food and beverage.  
We consider a measure of services necessity spending that includes but is not limited to groceries, utilities, and gasoline. Discretionary services 
includes but is not limited to charitable donations, leisure travel, entertainment, and professional/consumer services. Discretionary retail 
includes but is not limited to general merchandise, miscellaneous, clothing, electronics and furniture. It excludes categories like groceries and 
gasoline. Holiday spending is defined as items in which spending in the November-December period is usually at least 20% of total annual 
spending on the category.  Value and premium grocers are determined judgmentally on a proprietary analysis of merchants by equity analysts. 
The “more discretionary” retail category includes furniture, electronics, building materials, online marketplaces, clothing retailers and 
miscellaneous stores; while “partly discretionary” retail contains auto parts and services, health and personal care retailers, general merchandise 
and “mostly necessity”  retail contains gas and groceries. 
Lower, middle and higher household income cuts in Bank of America credit and debit card spending per household, and consumer deposit 
account data are based on quantitative estimates of each households’ income. These quantitative estimates are bucketed according to terciles, 
with a third of households placed in each tercile periodically. The lowest tercile represents ‘lower income’, the middle tercile represents ‘middle 
income’ and the highest tercile ‘higher income’. The income thresholds between these terciles will move over time, reflecting any number of 
factors that impact income, including general wage inflation, changes in social security payments and individual households’ income. The 
income and tercile in which a household is categorised are periodically re-assessed.  
Generations, if discussed, are defined as follows: Gen Z, born after 1995; Younger Millennials: born between 1989-1995; Older Millennials: born 
between 1978-1988; Gen Xers: born between 1965-1977; Baby Boomer: 1946-1964; Traditionalists: pre-1946. 
Any reference to card spending per household on gasoline includes all purchases at gasoline stations and might include purchases of non-gas 
items. 
Additional information about the methodology used to aggregate the data is available upon request.

## Document 320: Czech National Bank review_ The new neutral normal

Source PDF: all report pdf/Czech National Bank review_ The new neutral normal.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-11-05
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

The CNB increased the policy rate to 4%, in line with market
expectations. However, the decision was very close, a 4-3 split.
The new CNB forecast is revised toward a punchier inﬂation
amid a softer economic expansion, implying a higher rates
trajectory. We see 4% as the new normal, while further hikes
will be restrained by dwindling economic activity

David Havrlant and Frantisek Taborsky
In a split 4-3 vote, the Czech National Bank raised its main interest rate by
25bp to 4.00%
Punchier inﬂation amid mediocre economic
performance
The CNB proceeded with a rate hike on Thursday, raising the base
rate to 4%, which matches the expectations of market participants.
The decision was taken in a split vote as four board members
favoured a tighter monetary policy setup, while three policymakers
opted for rate stability. The situation is perceived as inﬂationary,
with persistently elevated core inﬂation, weaker exchange rate, and
a still strong housing market, along with ample credit growth, seen

Source: CNB, ING, Macrobond

The new CNB staﬀ projection points to slower economic expansion
along with more elevated inﬂation, as we have foreseen. We must
notice with some delight that the CNB inﬂation outlook is quite
close to ING's view throughout the forecast horizon. Meanwhile, the
CNB growth outlook remains a little optimistic compared with our
view, especially for the coming year. As for short-term interest rate,
the CNB forecast suggests a further increase up to 4.5% in the

nearest future, followed by a gradual decline from 2Q27 to 3.5% at
the end of 2028. We see such a trajectory rather as an alternative
scenario, should inﬂation prove even stronger than in our forecast.

The interesting thing is that the governor described the monetary
conditions as very tight at this point, and even beforehand. Well,
should our hypothesis about the need for much higher equilibrium
rates in the brave new world be right, then 4% seems to be a
neutral stance that just prevents inﬂation from systematically
hovering above the target. And with the CNB rate projection landing
at 3.5% at the very end of the forecasting horizon, we believe that

the staﬀ is perhaps starting to drift in the same direction, at least
implicitly.
That said, interest rates are an endogenous variable in the CNB
workhorse model, so you can argue that this particular rate path
enables the 2.5% economic expansion, with inﬂation receding
towards the target. But hey, would you really be cutting rated so
swiftly, while having inﬂation still running above your target and
the economy growing around its potential? I have some doubts,

but will stick to the old good maxim in dubio pro reo.

The infamous “all options are open” mantra was brought out of its
shallow grave and into the daylight once again, but the dwindling
real interest rate in the outlook does not suggest it stands on solid
ground. Overall, we see 4% as the new neutral normal and believe
that this will enable the CNB to ride out the wave of second-round
eﬀects, provided that our real GDP and inﬂation forecasts are right.
Our market view
The main takeaway from the CNB meeting is the 4–3 split

within the board, following a long period of strong majorities.
The market interprets this as a dovish signal, suggesting the
CNB is unwilling to hike rates further unless the inﬂation
situation deteriorates signiﬁcantly. For the ﬁrst time since the
onset of the US-Iran conﬂict, the CNB’s forecast aligns closely
with market pricing, with the PRIBOR rate peaking at around
4.55%. However, inﬂation is expected to worsen over the
remainder of the year, approaching 3% and nearing 4% in

the ﬁrst quarter of 2027, bringing the new CNB outlook closer
to our own forecast. We do not believe the current shift in
energy prices justiﬁes such a rally in CZK rates. The move
appears excessive at this juncture. Consequently, we view
two additional rate hikes as the minimum the market will

EUR/CZK is facing renewed upward pressure. Indeed, if we

toward 24.450. However, as previously noted, rates are likely
to correct, as the current rate diﬀerential is too tight.

temper the current weakening pressure following the dovish
CNB meeting. We therefore anticipate a new range of 24.350–
24.400, little changed from our pre-meeting expectations.

## Document 321: daily story_FLEX_2026_10_29_C_US

Source PDF: all report pdf/daily story_FLEX_2026_10_29_C_US.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-10-29
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

十月 29, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R  Powered by the EFA Platform 1

動態更新  硬體‧美國

Flex 
(FLEX.O/ FLEX US)

預期資料中心電源需求為獲利加速成長之主要動能 
重要訊息 
Flex FY2Q27 財報與 FY3Q27 財測均優於市場共識 ，公司上修 FY2027 資料
中心年增率自 70%至 80%，收盤股價上漲約 12%。 
評論及分析  
FY2Q27 資料中心電源需求升溫， EPS 年增 49%至 1.18 美元並優於共識。
Flex FY2Q27 (2026 年 7–9 月)營收年增 25%、季增 7%至 84.9 億美元，主
要來自資料中心電源 需求延續 ；受惠於電源產品營利率 年增 5.5ppt s 至約
16.0%、帶動產品組合轉佳， Flex FY2Q27  EPS 年增 49%、季增 18%至
1.18 美元，大致符合凱基原預估、優於市場共識 12%。 
FY3Q27 資料中心成長性可望優於 FY2Q27，預估 EPS 年增 52%至 1.32 美
元。Flex 已於 10 月完成對 EPC Power 的收購，該公司可望貢獻單季營收約
2 億美元，並有助於 800V 架構及固態變壓器( SST)的技術能力。FY 3Q27 隨
Hyperscalers 與 Neo clouds 等客戶需求延續，凱基預估 Flex 營收年增
33%、季增 11%至 93.9 億美元，其中資料中心業務之營收年增率預期達
115%，其餘產品線則約 10%上下；受惠營利率較佳的資料中心業務貢獻提
升，FY3Q27  EPS 預估年增 52%、季增 11%至 1.32 美元。 
凱基預估 Flex FY2027/FY2028  EPS 為 5.24/8.80 美元。基於電源產品接單
成長性優於預期以及併購 EPC Power 也將挹注營收，Flex 上修 FY2027 資料
中心營收年增率 自 70%至 80%，FY2028 則維持年增 80%之展望，隱含
FY2027/FY2028 資料中心營收分別上修 約 6% 、營收占比預期達
33%/45%。基於 Flex 上修展望，凱基 將 Flex FY2027/ FY2028 EPS 自原預估
之 4.96/7.96 美元上修至 5.24/8.80 美元，分別年增 59%/68%。 
投資建議  
凱基維持「增加持股」評等，目標價上修至 176 美元 (20x FY2028F EPS) 。 
投資風險  
關稅戰惡化導致客戶下單意願降低； CPI 業務營運成長態勢不如預期。

### Page 2

美國 Flex

十月 29, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R  2

Flex 資料中心營收占比提升至 30%。Flex 三大產品線為 ITS (包含網通與智慧
生活，FY 2Q27 營收占比 39%；營利率 5.5%)、RMS ( 包含工業、汽車與醫
療，FY1Q27 營收占比 31%；營利率 6.4%)、CPI ( 資料中心業務，FY1Q27
營收占比 30%；營利率 10.7%)。各項業務之應用主要包含:  (1) 工業產品涵
蓋資本設備、工業裝置、再生能源、電源與電網邊緣等解決方案； (2) 車用
產品主要提供下一代移動方案、自駕、連網化 、電動化與智慧科技 ；(3) 健
康解決方案包含醫療裝置、醫療設備與藥物輸送系統； (4) 智慧生活包含消
費性電子、家電、消費性包裝、掃地機器人、微型移動裝置及音訊產品； (5) 
網通領域涵蓋通訊基礎設施與企業網路傳輸； (6) 資料中心業務 包含電源、
散熱、系統整合等產品與服務。   
Flex 目前的發展策略是結合 EMS 與產品的服務策略以提升接單能力，公司
表示自身是全球唯一具有提供從資料中心晶片到電網能力的供應商，除了可
提供嵌入式電源 (embedded power) 產品、管理伺服器 GPU 的電源，亦可
滿足客戶對資料中心內所有設施的電力與散熱需求，提供完整的客製化服
務，帶動公司資料中心營收占比自前一季之 28%提升至 FY2Q27 之 30%。

Flex FY2027/ FY2028 資料中心營收與獲利成長性可望優於產業平均 。Flex
計劃將 CPI 部門分拆成一家新的上市公司 Axiom，維持在 CY1Q27 完成分拆
的進度不變， Flex 自身將專注於醫療保健、機器人、自動化倉儲和網通等產
業，兩家公司可更聚焦 於彼此的業務並提升資金運用效率。公司 提及自身在
資料中心電源布局相對完整，且產品價格亦相對具有競爭優勢下，使公司在
電源產品接單成長性優於產業平均； 隨包含 Google 在內的 Hyperscalers 、
Colocations 及 Neo-clouds 等客戶對嵌入式電源與 系統級解決方案的需求升
溫，公司在此次會議將 FY2027 資料中心營收年增率自 70%上修至 80%、
FY2028 則維持年增 80%之展望，皆較 FY2026 資料中心業務營收年增 38%
至 66 億美元加速成長 ，並隱含 FY2027/FY2028 資料中心營收分別上 修約
6%、營收占比預期達 33%/45%。

### Page 3

美國 Flex

十月 29, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R  3

獲利性部分， 受惠於電源產品營利率較佳、 FY2Q27 年增 5.5ppts 至約
16.0%，優於資料中心業務的平均營利率約 11%以及公司整體平均營利率
約 7%，公司上修 FY2027 資料中心業務之營利率約 1ppt 至 11.6%。凱基
預估隨資料中心營收貢獻提升， FY2027/FY2028 營 利占比分別達
49%/65%，為公司主要獲利動能。  
至於資料中心以外之 其餘應用，預期營收年增率 維持約 10%上下之水準。
目前歐洲工業需求略 有回溫，營收年增率可望維持約 10%上下；汽車產品
線則因目前全球汽車銷售量已逐步回升至過往 9,000 萬輛的正常水準，
FY2027 /FY2028 產品線成長性預期落在 5%以內；醫療設備則因客戶先前在
Covid -19 時期堆積的庫存水位過高，目前庫存去化雖逐步落底，但 需求端仍
相對平緩；網通領域則預期隨庫存去化力道放緩， FY2027 產品線營收將逐
步回溫，預期年增率 約 10%；消費性電子則因記憶體漲價、品牌廠將成本
轉嫁給消費者的影響， FY202 7 營收預期年減約 5-10%。 
Flex 評等維持「增加持股」，目標價上修至 176 美元。基於 Flex 上修資料
中心之營收與營 利率展望，凱基將 Flex FY2027/FY2028 EPS 自原預估之
4.96/7.96 美元上修至 5.24/8.80 美元，分別年增 59%/68%；相較市場共識
FY202 7/FY2028  EPS 預估為 4.48/6.82 美元，股價交易於約 12/14 倍之
FY2028 凱基與市場共識 EPS 預估。 
Flex 過往五年本益比多落於 5-20 倍之間，考量 (1) 公司在資料中心領域掌握
從晶片、液冷散熱、BBU 到電網的供應能力 ，布局相對多數同業更全面 ；(2) 
公司持續提升在 CSP 廠供應鏈之接單量，有助 FY2027 /FY2028 營收與獲利
加速成長； (3) 基於資料中心產品線營利率可望逐步提升至 10-15%、顯著
優於公司過往產品之 5%水準，且在獲利占比逐步提升至 50%以上的帶動
下，有助公司評價向散熱與電源同業靠攏。綜合上述 原因，評估 Flex 本益比
可望回升至區間 上緣之 20 倍上下浮動 ；基於 Flex 當前評價偏低 ，故維持
「增加持股」之投資評等，目標價隨 EPS 預估上修而調整至 176 美元 (20x 
FY202 8F EPS)。

### Page 7

美國 Flex

十月 29, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R 7

項目 定義 
能源使用 
直接與間接能源消耗總量(十億焦耳) 
- 公司運營範圍內消耗之能源總量 
- 能源使用總量 = 直接能源消耗總量 + 間接能源消耗量 
- 總能源使用量包括購買的能源、生產的能源 
- 就公用事業而言，輸電/電網損耗為其業務活動之一部分，被視為總能源消耗，數據不包括為滿足能源使用而生產的電力（公用
事業為出售而生產） 
- 就公用事業而言，用於能源生產的煤、天然氣或核能等原料不屬於「總能源使用」項下 
購買之再生能源 
Primary再生能源購買總量(十億焦耳) 
- 公司各種來源之能源消耗量與購買的能源中屬於自然界可再生者（太陽能、風能、水能、生物質能、地熱能）之量  
- 如無證據顯示再生能源由公司生產，則所報告的能源數據視為購買的再生能源 
再生能源使用率 再生能源占總能源消耗量之比例 
CO2 約當排放量 
直接 CO2 與 CO2 約當排放量(公噸) 
- 公司擁有或控制的來源的直接排放量（範圍 1 排放量） 
- 相關氣體：二氧化碳 (CO2)、甲烷 (CH4)、一氧化二氮 (N2O)、氫氟碳化物 (HFCS)、全氟化合物 (PFCS)、六氟化硫 (SF6)、三氟化氮 
(NF3) 
CO2 約當排放量營收比 
直接 CO2 與 CO2 約當排放量（公噸）銷售（百萬元）比 
- 公司擁有或控制的來源的直接排放量（範圍 1 排放量） 
- 相關氣體：二氧化碳 (CO2)、甲烷 (CH4)、一氧化二氮 (N2O)、氫氟碳化物 (HFCS)、全氟化合物 (PFCS)、六氟化硫 (SF6)、三氟化氮 
(NF3) 
廢棄物總量 
廢棄物總量(公噸) 
- 總廢棄物 = 無害廢棄物 + 有害廢棄物 
- 僅考慮固體廢棄物，但如液體廢棄物以公噸為單位呈報，則會將其加入求得包含液體廢棄物之總量 
- 對於採礦、石油與天然氣等行業，tailings、廢石、煤、飛灰等廢棄物亦考慮在內 
廢棄物回收率 
公司呈報的廢棄物回收率 
- 廢棄物回收率 = 廢棄物回收量/總廢棄物*100 
- 廢棄物轉化為能源或經由廢棄物焚燒產生能源視為廢棄物回收 
- 經由堆肥回收的廢棄物視為回收之廢棄物 
總取水量 
總取水量(立方公尺) 
- 由呈報組織直接或經水公司等中介機構從任何水源抽取的總水量 
- 水井、城鎮/公用事業/市政用水、河水、地表水等不同之水源均予以考慮 
環保支出 環保支出總金額 - 所有用於環境保護，或防止、減少、控制環境因素、影響、危害的投資與支出，亦包括處置、處理、衛生、清理支出 
員工流動率 
員工流動率 
- 包括任何原因（自願或非自願）離開公司的員工，如辭職、退休、自然離職/死亡、醫療失能、冗員、裁員、重組、解僱、裁減
或定期合約到期 
- 員工流動率 =（離開之員工/平均員工人數）*100 
- 平均員工人數 =（本年末員工人數 + 去年末員工人數）/2 
- 本財年末員工數 =上個財年末員工數 + 新員工數 - 離開之員工數 
女性經理 
女性經理百分比 
- 女性經理占公司經理的百分比 
- 如有不同階層之百分比，如最高階、高階、中階、初階，則會考慮中階女性經理之百分比 
- 女性經理百分比 = 女性經理人數/經理總數*100 
女性員工 
女性員工百分比 
- 女性員工占公司員工總數的百分比 
- 女性員工百分比 = 女性人數/員工總數*100 
教育訓練總時數 
所有員工教育訓練總時數 
- 僅考慮員工教育訓練時數 
- 包括一般員工所有類型的教育訓練（如健康與安全、環境、急難事件救援、技能與職業發展） 
- 如果數據以天為單位，則乘以 8，係假設 1 天 =  8 小時工作 
每位員工教育訓練時數 平均每年每位員工總教育訓練時數 
股東治理分數 衡量公司用以反收購工具的有效性 
公司治理分數 衡量公司對最佳治理原則的承諾和有效性 
產品責任分數 衡量公司生產優質產品和服務的能力，且產品是否將客戶的健康、安全、整合性和數據隱私進行綜合考量 
社區關係分數 衡量公司對成為優良公民、保護公眾健康和尊重商業道德的承諾 
勞動力分數 衡量公司在員工工作滿意度、健康、工作場所的安全、多樣性、平等以及員工發展機會方面的成效 
資源使用指標 衡量公司在原物料、能源或水的使用效率，以及是否通過改進供應鏈來尋求更俱生態效率的解決方案

## Document 365: Intel出事它卻沒事？營收上升但毛利下降？ 財報解剖_ AMD_ 26年11月

Source PDF: all report pdf/Intel出事它卻沒事？營收上升但毛利下降？ 財報解剖_ AMD_ 26年11月.pdf
Report source: researchmail轉寄
Date: 2026-11-04
Pages: 23
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

From:    KP - FOMO 研究院  <fomosoc@substack.com>
Subject: Intel 出事它卻沒事？營收上升但⽑利下降？  財報解剖 : AMD| 26 年 11 ⽉
Date:    3 hours ago
Intel 出事它卻沒事？營收上升但⽑利下降？  財報解剖 : AMD| 26 年 11 ⽉
View in browser
Intel 出事它卻沒事？營收上升但⽑利
下降？  財報解剖 : AMD| 26 年 11 ⽉
KP@FOMOSOC AND BENITA HSU
NOV 4 ∙ PAID
READ IN APP
10 ⽉ 6 ⽇到 11 ⽉ 3 ⽇財報公布前， AMD 的股價從 649.42 美元⼀路跌到 483.90 美
元，不到⼀個⽉摔掉了 165.52 美元（跌幅 25.5% ）。
光是 10 ⽉ 30 ⽇ Intel 發出 PC 賣不動的警訊那天， AMD 單⽇就被拖累⼤跌近
11% 。
財報都還沒出來，市場已經先把最壞的劇本演了⼀遍。

### Page 2

結果 11 ⽉ 3 ⽇盤後財報⼀開牌， AMD 盤後股價⽴刻反彈。
字⾯上的成績單確實漂亮：
第三季總營收年增 45% 來到 134 億美元，第四季營收指引更直接喊到 170 億美
元（年增 66% ），其中「資料中⼼」部⾨第三季營收年增 97% ，幾乎翻倍。

### Page 3

執⾏⻑蘇姿丰更在法說會上預告， 2027 年全公司總營收將再成⻑超過 80% 。
但翻開財報細項，卻會看到兩個耐⼈尋味的反差：
明明營收與獲利都在創紀錄，第三季真正流進⼝袋的「⾃由現⾦流」卻⼤
減了 64% ；⽽且為了綁定⼤客戶所送出的股票（認股權證），還在帳上砸出
了近 11 億美元的業外損失。
這份財報真正要回答的問題是：
財報前跌掉的那 25% ，有多少是市場錯殺？
當 AMD 準備邁向近千億美元的營收規模，這⾨⼤⽣意背後的真實利潤與現⾦
流究竟⻑什麼樣⼦？
接下來，我們就⽤最⽩話的⽅式，把這場轉變背後的財務邏輯⼀步步算清楚。

### Page 4

重點⼀｜ Helios 系統正式出貨：為什
麼資料中⼼賣越多，⽑利率反⽽微降？
1.1 什麼是 Helios ？只賣「核⼼晶⽚」與「設計圖」的⽣意模式
過去買晶⽚，客戶買回去⾃⼰插上伺服器就能⽤。
但現在的 AI 運算太龐⼤，客戶需要的是⼀整座已經幫你把上百顆晶⽚、網路線
與散熱系統全部串聯好的「⼤型機櫃」。
AMD 推出的 Helios ，就是這樣⼀套「整座機櫃的設計藍圖」。
特別的是， AMD ⾃⼰只負責賣機櫃裡最昂貴的核⼼⼤腦（包含 Instinct AI 晶
⽚、 EPYC 處理器、網路晶⽚與軟體），⾄於外⾯的⾦屬機殼、電源供應器與整
機組裝，則交給像 HPE 、 Dell 、 Super Micro 這類品牌伺服器⼤廠與系統夥伴去
出貨並向客戶收費。
這代表 AMD 不必賺利潤微薄的鐵櫃與組裝費，帳⾯認列的營收主要來⾃⾼單價
的核⼼晶⽚⽅案。
不過要注意的是，每⼀顆 AI 晶⽚裡都封裝了向記憶體⼤廠採購的⾼昂 HBM
（⾼頻寬記憶體），這筆硬體採購成本會直接算進 AMD 的銷貨成本裡；再加上

為了搶⼤單所給出的價格優惠，都會影響最終的⽑利率表現。
蘇姿丰在法說會上證實，新⼀代 MI455X 晶⽚已在第三季⼤量出貨，包括
Anthropic 、 Meta 、 Microsoft 、 OpenAI 與 Oracle 都已經讓第⼀批初期的 Helios 系
統上線運作並跑起模型。
到了第四季， AI 晶⽚的營收更將在歷史上⾸度超越傳統伺服器 CPU 。
這也帶動資料中⼼部⾨展現強⼤的賺錢效率：
第三季資料中⼼部⾨營業利益年增 168% 來到 29 億美元，相當於每做 100 美元⽣
意，就有約 34 美元是營業利益（較去年同期的 25 美元⼤幅提升）。
1.2 算⼀筆明細帳：新增資料中⼼營收的五成⽑利秘密
既然核⼼晶⽚這麼熱銷，為什麼 AMD 預估第四季全公司的⽑利率會從 56% 微
幅降到 55% ？

### Page 5

道理很像⼀家便當店接到了⼤型企業的團膳⼤單：
雖然為了搶下⼤單，每個便當的單價壓低了⼀點、⾷材成本也⾼，讓平均⽑利
率微幅下降，但因為⼀次賣出好幾千個，收銀機進帳的總獲利反⽽迎來⼤爆
發。
我們可以把第四季的財測數字拆開來，算算看 AMD 多賣的這批資料中⼼訂單
到底賺多少⽑利：
先算資料中⼼多賣了多少錢：
第四季總營收預計為  170 億美元，⽐第三季多出約  36 億美元。
但別忘了，第三季貢獻  37 億美元的「 PC 與電競部⾨」，第四季預計會衰退超
過  20% （⾄少少掉  7.4 億美元）。
⼀邊少賺、⼀邊還要讓總營收⼤增，算下來資料中⼼第四季營收必須單季暴增
約  43 億美元，相當於⼀個季度就⻑⼤了  50% 。

### Page 6

再算這批新增訂單的⽑利率：
第四季預估總⽑利約  93.5 億美元（ 170 億  × 55% ），⽐第三季多出  18.7 億美
元。
假設少掉的那  7.4 億美元  PC 業務⽑利率⼤約是  50% （此為推算假設），回推那
43 億美元新增的資料中⼼營收，每多賣  100 美元⼤約只能賺到  50 美元的⽑利
（邊際⽑利率粗估約  50% ）。
NVIDIA 整體的平均⽑利率⻑期⾼達 70% 以上，⽽ AMD 第四季度新增資料
中⼼營收（包含 AI 晶⽚與伺服器 CPU ）的邊際⽑利率粗估約落在 50% 左
右。
這組數字除了反映⾼昂的 HBM 記憶體成本外，也把「 AMD ⽤價格折扣與送
股票換取市占率」變成了⼀個具體可追蹤的財務指標。
當然，因為單季多賣了 43 億美元，規模放⼤的威⼒依然驚⼈，第四季全公司的
營業利益預計將從第三季的 39 億美元⼀路跳升⾄約 56 億美元。
重點⼆｜管理層喊出 2027 成⻑破
80% ：要達標，你得相信什麼？
2.1 短短 90 天內，再度⼤幅調⾼明年⽬標

⽐起第三季成績單，這場法說會真正的重頭戲，是管理層⼤幅調⾼了 2027 年的
成⻑⽬標。
蘇姿丰表⽰，過去 90 天內多家雲端巨頭擴⼤了採購計畫。
對⽐ 8 ⽉份法說會時管理層預估「明年伺服器 CPU 成⻑超過 70% 」，這次直接上
修為「明年伺服器 CPU 營收將接近翻倍」，資料中⼼ AI 晶⽚營收更將「成⻑遠
超過兩倍（ grow significantly more than twofold ）」，帶動資料中⼼佔 2027 年全公
司總營收達到 80% 以上。

### Page 7

更重要的是，管理層指出 2027 年伺服器 CPU 營收翻倍的動能來⾃三⼤塊：
傳統雲端與企業端汰換、 AI 代理（ Agentic AI ）⼯作負載，以及 AI 叢集的主節
點（ Head Nodes ）。
隨著新⼀代 EPYC Venice 處理器在第三季開始出貨（合作夥伴承諾開發的平台
數超過 240 個，⽐上⼀代多出兩成），這波 CPU 成⻑主要是由「出貨量增加」與
「⾼核⼼數產品組合」所驅動，⽽⾮單純靠漲價，這意味著企業與雲端升級
CPU 的需求⽐市場想像得更加紮實。
2.2 邁向 940 億美元的算術題：真正的上限在⼯廠產能
「 2027 年總營收成⻑超過 80% 」換算成⾦額有多⼤？
2026 年全年營收基準： 2026 年前三季營收加上第四季預估的  170 億美
元，今年全年總營收約為  521 億美元。

### Page 8

2027 年營收⽬標： 明年再成⻑超過  80% ，代表  2027 全年營收將⼀舉突破
939 億美元。這個數字已經追平了外資  Bernstein 先前最樂觀的預估（ 938
億美元），更超越了  Morgan Stanley 的預期（ 889 億美元）。
但要做到近 940 億美元並不容易。
就算把第四季創紀錄的 170 億美元直接乘以 4 個季度，⼀年也才 680 億美元，中
間還有 260 億美元的缺⼝。
這代表在 2027 年裡， AMD 每⼀季營收平均都得⽐上⼀季再⻑⼤ 13% 到 14% ，且
到了 2027 年第四季，單季營收必須衝上約 280 億美元。
蘇姿丰在問答環節坦⾔⽬前「訂單需求⼤於供給」。這說明 2027 年營收能不
能衝上 940 億美元，關鍵在於 AMD 能從供應商⼿上搶到多少⾼階記憶體與
晶⽚封裝產能。
重點三｜買晶⽚送股票：拆解「認股權
證」的代價與財報怪象
3.1 算算看送股票的代價： Meta 的領股條件⽐ OpenAI 更寬鬆
為了吸引雲端巨頭採⽤⾃家產品， AMD 先前與 OpenAI 及 Meta 簽署協議，分別

給予最⾼ 1.6 億股（合計 3.2 億股）、每股只需要花 0.01 美元就能認購的「附條
件認股權證」。
為什麼要送這麼⼤禮？
因為⼤客戶從 NVIDIA 換成 AMD ，得派⼤批⼯程師重寫軟體、重新設計機房，
轉換成本極⾼。
但仔細對⽐兩家巨頭的合約細節，會發現條件⼤不相同：
OpenAI （綁定採購量＋股價⾨檻）：
OpenAI 的  1.6 億股權證採分批解鎖，除了要買夠晶⽚，最後⼀批還規定要等
AMD 股價漲到  600 美元才能領⾛。
如果  OpenAI 最終全數領滿，這批股票的市值⾄少⾼達  960 億美元（ 1.6 億股  ×
600 美元）。
Meta （只綁採購與出貨⾥程碑，無股價⾨檻）：

### Page 9

Meta 的條件更為寬鬆。同樣最多可領  1.6 億股（搭配  5 年最⾼  600 億美元的採
購承諾），但只看採購量與出貨⾥程碑，沒有設定股價⾨檻。
換句話說，就算  AMD 股價原地踏步，只要  Meta 買夠多晶⽚，股票⼀樣拿得
到。
⽤⽬前的股數換算，兩家巨頭最多各⾃可以拿到 AMD ⼤約⼀成的股份（若 3.2
億股全數發出，現有股東的持股⽐例將被稀釋約 16.0% ）。
這筆交易的本質，就是拿原有股東的股權，去補貼⼤客戶轉換平台的建置成
本。
3.2 財報上的反直覺怪象：五成⽑利背後的「折價券」與業外損
失
你可能會好奇，為什麼第三季 AMD 官⽅財報（ GAAP ）的每股盈餘只有 1.46 美
元，但調整後（ Non-GAAP ）卻⾼達 2.09 美元，中間差了 0.63 美元？
翻開財報調節表，除了常⾒的員⼯股票薪酬（ 5.23 億美元）與併購攤銷（ 5.34
億美元）以及所得稅調整的抵銷（約負 10.73 億美元，即每股抵銷 0.64 美元）
之外，單⼀最⼤的⼀塊調整項，就來⾃這批承諾送給客戶的認股權證：

### Page 10

當作折價券直接扣營收（拉低⽑利率的隱形推⼿）：
根據會計規則，當  AMD 開始向  OpenAI 與  Meta 出貨時，送出去的權證價值會
按⽐例當作給客戶的「銷售折扣（營收折讓）」，直接從營收裡扣掉。
這正好解開了重點⼀的謎團：為什麼第四季新增資料中⼼營收的邊際⽑利率只
有  50% 左右？
因為裡⾯有⼀部分利潤，其實就是以「送股票當折價券」的形式讓利給了⼤客
戶。
每季重新計價的「⽋條」損失：
在客戶還沒真正把權證換成股票之前，會計規則把這批承諾要給的股票視為
AMD ⽋客戶的「負債（⽋條）」，⽽且每⼀季都要按照最新股價與出貨進度重
新計算這張⽋條值多少錢。
第三季因為  AMD 股價上漲、出貨順利，這張⽋條跟著變貴了，導致  AMD 必
須在官⽅損益表上認列⾼達  10.97 億美元（每股  0.66 美元）的業外帳⾯損失。

### Page 11

這會造成⼀個⾮常反直覺的財報現象：未來只要 AMD 晶⽚賣得越好、股價
漲得越⾼，官⽅ GAAP 損益表上的「帳⾯損失」反⽽會跟著變⼤。
（⾄於實際的股數增加與稀釋，財務⻑表⽰要等 2027 年客戶陸續達成出貨
條件並換成股票時才會發⽣。）
3.3 怎麼判斷客戶是不是衝著送股票來的？把名單分成兩組看
既然送股票的代價這麼⾼，我們該怎麼確認客戶買 AMD 是因為產品真的好
⽤？最實際的做法，是把⼤客戶分成兩組來追蹤：
有附帶條件的客戶： OpenAI 與  Meta （拿到認股權證，且  AMD 近期也宣
布投資  Meta 10 億美元），以及  Anthropic （合約中包含  AMD 反過來投資
Anthropic 的承諾）。
純商業採購的客戶（無附帶條件）： Microsoft 、 Oracle ，以及這次新宣
布、透過  HPE 出貨給雲端服務商  Vultr 的  12 億美元  Helios 機櫃⼤單。

### Page 12

追蹤 Microsoft 、 Oracle 與 Vultr 這類「不拿免費股票也願意掏錢」的客戶訂單⽐
重，會是接下來檢驗 AMD 真實產品定價權最直接的溫度計。
重點四｜同樣遇到 PC 賣不動，為什麼
AMD 沒像 Intel ⼀樣暴雷？
4.1 伺服器 CPU 都在⼤賺，差別在於「包袱有多重」
10 ⽉底 Intel 發出財報警訊時，市場⼀度擔⼼ AMD 也會跟著遭殃。
但其實翻開 Intel 財報， Intel 第三季伺服器 CPU 出貨量年增了 10% ，平均售價更
⼤漲 54% ，帶動資料中⼼營收⼤增 78% 來到 73 億美元。
Intel 真正的痛點有兩個：

### Page 13

第⼀， PC 業務仍佔它總營收將近⼀半（ 81 億美元），當 PC 買氣下滑，⽴刻把第
四季總營收指引拖累到 155 億美元；
第⼆， Intel ⾃⼰養了昂貴的晶圓廠，不管⼯廠有沒有訂單，每季都得付龐⼤的
機器折舊與維護費，⼀旦 PC 銷量變少，產能利⽤率下滑的⼯廠就會嚴重吃掉
利潤。
4.2 AMD 的兩⼤優勢：逾六成營收靠資料中⼼、晶圓產能靈活
調度
AMD 同樣沒躲過記憶體漲價與 PC 買氣降溫。
第三季 PC 與電競部⾨營收年減 8% 來到 37 億美元，第四季更預計衰退超過
20% ；管理層甚⾄在法說會上坦⾔，因為記憶體與零組件漲價壓抑終端買氣，
內部在規劃 2027 年財務⽬標時，已經直接假設「 2027 年全球 PC 出貨量將出現
雙位數百分⽐（ 10% 以上）的衰退」。

### Page 14

但即便把這麼悲觀的 PC 假設放進模型裡， AMD 卻依然能交出明年總營收成⻑
破 80% 的超預期指引，關鍵就在於兩⼤體質差異：
資料中⼼已經⻑成絕對主⼒：
AMD 的資料中⼼營收佔⽐已從去年的  47% 拉升到⽬前的  64% （第四季將逼近
75% ，明年更將超過  80% ），靠著伺服器  CPU 與  AI 晶⽚雙引擎，輕鬆填補了
PC 的缺⼝。
把台積電晶圓轉去做⾼⽑利產品：
雖然  AMD 對台積電也有產能採購承諾，但因為台積電的⾼階晶圓產能⾮常吃
緊，當  PC 需求變弱時，蘇姿丰在法說會上指出， AMD 剛好可以把原本要做
PC 的晶圓與載板封裝產能，靈活調度去做⽑利更⾼、供不應求的資料中⼼產
品。
Intel 則剛好相反， PC ⼀弱，⾃家⼯廠的產能利⽤率就跟著掉。

### Page 15

同樣遇到  PC 逆⾵， AMD 是在升級產品組合， Intel 則是被迫吸收龐⼤的⼯
廠固定成本。
重點五｜搶產能與拼軟體：記憶體漲價
下， ROCm 10 扮演什麼⾓⾊？
5.1 記憶體⼤廠開始漲價，成本誰來吸收？
前⾯提到，決定 AMD 2027 年能否衝上 940 億美元的關鍵在於供應鏈產能。
每⼀顆 AI 晶⽚裡都必須搭配⾼頻寬記憶體（ HBM ），⽽記憶體⼤廠 SK hynix 已
經宣布與 14 家⼤客戶簽下⻑約，把 2027 到 2028 年過半的產能都預售光了，還
加⼊了隨市場⾏情調漲價格的條款。
當上游產能被搶光，記憶體廠⾃然掌握了漲價話語權。
蘇姿丰在法說會上坦承零組件確實⾯臨漲價壓⼒，⽬前正與客戶協商「共同分
攤成本」。
這筆多出來的硬體成本，最終有多少能轉嫁給雲端巨頭、有多少得由 AMD ⾃
⼰吸收，將直接影響明年的⽑利率表現。
5.2 為什麼軟體是破局關鍵？幫客戶省下「⼯程師的加班費」
在硬體成本變貴的背景下， AMD 推出最新 ROCm 10 軟體系統的重要性就更加

凸顯了。
我們站在雲端客戶的⾓度想⼀想：
買晶⽚硬體只是第⼀步。如果你買 AMD 的晶⽚省了 20% 硬體費，卻得派幾百
名⾼薪⼯程師花三個⽉改寫程式、解決當機問題才能讓機房順利運轉，那浪費
掉的時間與⼯程師薪⽔，早就把省下的硬體錢吃光了。
ROCm 10 主打讓⼤型機櫃買回去就能快速啟動、⾃動調校效能，⽬的就是把
客戶的「使⽤⾨檻與⿇煩程度」降到最低。
這也是為什麼 AMD 在第三季同步出⼿完成了兩筆關鍵收購：
⼀是收購由 AI 視覺教⺟李⾶⾶創⽴的空間智慧新創 World Labs ，並延攬李⾶⾶
直接出任 AMD ⾸席科學家，從最前沿的模型研發端開始與硬體及 ROCm 軟體
深度綁定；

### Page 16

⼆是收購客製化極速推論晶⽚新創 Taalas ，強化超低延遲的推論能⼒。
只要軟體與模型⽣態系越好⽤、幫客戶省下的⼯程師時間與維護成本越
多， AMD 就越不需要靠硬體殺價或送股票來搶⽣意。
重點六｜帳⾯⼤賺 35 億，為什麼進⼝
袋的現⾦卻少掉六成？
6.1 拆解 33 億美元的「做⽣意墊款」
第三季 AMD 在調整後的損益表上⼤賺了 34.7 億美元，但真正流進公司⼝袋、
可以⾃由動⽤的現⾦（⾃由現⾦流）卻只有 5.54 億美元。
無論是跟上⼀季的 15.58 億美元、還是去年同期的 15.30 億美元相⽐，都⼤幅縮
⽔了 64% 。

### Page 17

第三季買設備與蓋廠房的資本⽀出為 6.48 億美元（⽐第⼆季的 8.08 億美元還
少），真正把現⾦吸⾛的，是⾼達 33 億美元的「⽇常做⽣意墊款」（營運資⾦淨
流出）。
要做近千億美元的⼤⽣意，在收到客戶貨款前， AMD 得先在三個地⽅⾃掏腰包
墊錢：
買原料囤貨（多壓了近  30 億美元）：
倉庫裡的存貨總額攀升到  115 億美元（平均要放  169 天才能賣出，⽐上季的
145 天更久），主要是為了第四季與明年的  MI455 晶⽚及  Helios 系統提前備
料。
付訂⾦鎖產能（多付了約  10 億美元）：
預付款項增加了  10 億美元。對照前⾯  SK hynix 要求簽⻑約鎖產能，這筆錢很
⼤⼀部分就是為了幫  2027 年搶下記憶體與封裝產能所付出的現⾦訂⾦。
客戶拿了貨還沒付錢（應收帳款多了約  19 億美元）：
貨已經出給客戶並算進營收了，但客戶平均要  63 天後才會把錢匯過來（⽐上
季的  57 天拉⻑）。
值得注意的是，現在買  AI 晶⽚的除了  Microsoft 、 Meta 這種現⾦滿⼿的巨頭

外，也包含⼀部分還在⼤量燒錢的  AI 新創實驗室，未來這筆帳款能不能準時
收回，值得持續追蹤。

### Page 18

把上⾯三項加起來，流出⾦額⾼達約 59 億美元；扣掉 AMD ⾃⼰延後付給供應
商的貨款（應付帳款與其他負債增加約 26 億美元）之後，算下來的營運資⾦淨
流出就是 33 億美元。
6.2 公司不缺錢，但算估值時要把「墊款成本」算進去
財務⻑已經預告，為了⽀撐明年的⾼成⻑， 2027 年還是得繼續掏現⾦買庫存、
鎖產能。
不過⼤家不⽤擔⼼ AMD 會資⾦周轉不靈。
截⾄第三季末， AMD 帳上躺著⾼達 158 億美元的現⾦與短期投資，⽽借款（總
債務）只有 71 億美元（其中包含今年 8 ⽉為了提前儲備營運糧草所發⾏的 47.5
億美元公司債），財務家底⾮常厚實。

### Page 19

損益表上的 EPS 告訴你東⻄賣掉後帳⾯賺多少；⽽⾃由現⾦流則誠實地提醒
我們，為了撐起這場千億美元的營收爆發， AMD 必須先掏出多少現⾦去墊
款備料。
重點七｜從⼤跌 25% 到盤後反彈：現
在約 510 美元的 AMD 到底貴不貴？
7.1 現在的股價到底在反映什麼？
財報公布前，外資對 AMD 2027 年 EPS 的預估平均約 15.7 美元。以這個數字計
算， 10 ⽉初 649.42 美元的股價約是 41.4 倍本益⽐，財報前跌到 483.90 美元時約
是 30.8 倍。

### Page 20

現在管理層喊出 2027 年營收成⻑超過 80% ，我們重算⼀次：
營收與⽑利： 營收抓  940 億美元，⽑利率約  54% ，總⽑利約  508 億美
元。
營業費⽤給兩種假設： 營收成⻑  80% ，費⽤不必跟著漲這麼多，但  AMD
每年都要推新⼀代晶⽚、加碼軟體投資，費⽤也不會原地不動。樂觀⼀點
抓  175 億美元（成⻑約  25% ），保守⼀點抓  190 億美元（成⻑約  35% ）。
淨利： 扣掉  13% 稅率，全年淨利約落在  276 億到  289 億美元。
每股盈餘： 如果把明年部分解鎖的客戶權證算進去（假設股數增加到約
17.6 億股）， EPS ⼤約是  15.7 到  16.4 美元。
有趣的是，費⽤抓 190 億美元時算出的 15.7 美元，幾乎和財報前的市場共識⼀
模⼀樣。 Bernstein 先前預估營收 938 億美元， EPS 也只有 15.82 美元。
營收⽬標往上調了⼀⼤截，每股盈餘卻沒跟著動多少。多出來的⽣意，被五成
左右的邊際⽑利、送出去的股票和增加的費⽤分掉了。

### Page 21

拿 15.7 到 16.4 美元對照盤後約 510 美元的股價，遠期本益⽐⼤約是 31 到 32.5
倍。
本益⽐之外，還有⼀個數字要⼀起看。
現在 AMD 市值約 8,540 億美元，如果 2027 年淨利 280 億美元能有⼋成變成⾃由
現⾦流，股價約是⾃由現⾦流的 38 倍；如果只有五成，就變成 61 倍。第三季
這個⽐例只有 16% 。
所以現在的 510 美元，⼤致是「相信 940 億美元營收會達標，也相信明年的
現⾦會跟著回來」。
營收和現⾦這兩件事少了任何⼀件，現價都不算便宜。
7.2 給讀者的三個估值參考座標

### Page 22

AMD 現在訂單⽐產能多，所以明年真正的變數是能拿到多少記憶體與封裝產
能、⽤什麼成本出貨。
以下三個情境都⽤上⾯同⼀套公式計算，再搭配不同的本益⽐。（註：此為測
試獲利與本益⽐變化的算術情境，並⾮⽬標價預測。）
保守情境（產能卡關、記憶體漲價吃掉⽑利）： 營收  880 億美元、⽑利率
53.5% 、費⽤  185 億美元， EPS 約  14.3 美元。客戶買得少，解鎖的權證也
少，稀釋較輕。乘以  25 倍，約  357 美元。
基準情境（產能⼤致到位、達標  940 億美元）： EPS 約  15.7 到  16.4 美
元，乘以  31 倍，約  487 到  510 美元，⼤致就是⽬前的盤後股價。
樂觀情境（拿到更多產能、成本成功轉嫁給客戶）： 營收  1,000 億美元、
⽑利率  56% 、費⽤  180 億美元， EPS 約  18.6 美元。這時客戶買得多，解
鎖的權證也多，股數假設約  17.8 億股。乘以  35 倍，約  650 美元，剛好回
到  10 ⽉初的⾼點。

樂觀情境裡有個⼩諷刺： AMD 賣得越好，送出去的股票就越多。
結論
回頭看財報前那 25% （約 165 美元）的跌幅，我們可以⽤⽂章裡的數字拆成兩
塊來算。
第⼀塊是 PC 恐慌。
事實上，管理層在給出「明年總營收成⻑破 80% 」的⽬標時，內部早就已經把
「 2027 年全球 PC 出貨量衰退雙位數百分⽐」的悲觀預期算進去了。
就算我們假設 2027 年 PC 與電競部⾨再多衰退兩成，少掉的營收⼤約是 24 到 28
億美元（以第四季單季約 30 億美元推算），在 50% ⽑利率與 13% 稅率假設下，
換算成 EPS ⼤約只影響 0.7 美元。
⽤ 30 倍本益⽐計算，⼤概只值 20 美元出頭的股價；就算⽤財報前 41 倍的本益
⽐來算，也不到 30 美元。
Intel 暴雷那天 AMD 單⽇跌掉近 11% ，跌幅遠遠超過 PC 業務真正能造成的傷
害。
第⼆塊是本益⽐從 41 倍回到 30 倍左右，這⼀段反⽽有它的道理。

### Page 23

要讓  649 美元的股價在 30 倍本益⽐下站得住腳， AMD 2027 年的 EPS 得做到
21.6 美元左右；⽤同樣的營收與費⽤往回推，全公司⽑利率必須拉⾼到 63% 上
下。
但這份財報透露的⽅向剛好相反：新增資料中⼼營收的邊際⽑利率⼤約五成、
送給 OpenAI 與 Meta 的權證會稀釋股本並折減營收，搶產能⼜持續壓著現⾦
流。
營收的⾼成⻑已經被證實了，但賺錢的品質還需要時間證明。
⽽這被殺的估值倍數未來能不能再賺回來，就要看接下來這  4 個核⼼指
標：
1. Q4 資料中⼼爆發⼒： 第四季資料中⼼營收能否如期單季⼤增  50% （多賣
約  43 億美元）？
2. ⽑利率防線： 在邊際⽑利約  50% 的新訂單放量、權證折扣攤提，以及記
憶體漲價的分攤協商下，全公司⽑利率能否穩守  55% ？
3. 純商業訂單的拓展： Microsoft 、 Oracle 與  Vultr 這類「不拿免費股票」的
標準訂單，佔⽐能否持續擴⼤？
4. 現⾦回收的速度： 壓在倉庫裡的  115 億美元存貨與  19 億美元新增應收帳

款，明年能否順利變成現⾦流回⼝袋？
AMD 已經向市場證明了⾃⼰搶下近千億營收規模的實⼒；⽽在下⼀個階段，股
價能不能重回前⾼，將取決於這場盛宴過後，最終有多少利潤能真正轉化為⾃
由現⾦流，留進股東的⼝袋裡。

## Document 415: September CPI_ Inflation accelerating in the run up to Christmas (2026-11-05)

Source PDF: all report pdf/September CPI_ Inflation accelerating in the run up to Christmas (2026-11-05).pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 18
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#inflation #australia #rba #economicbulletinsaustralia #inflation
September CPI: Inflation accelerating in the run up to Christmas
By Justin Smirk & Neha Sharma
16:08 October 28 2026 Share
September Monthly CPI: Headline 0.8%mth/4.3%yr, Trimmed Mean 0.3.%mth/3.6%yr.
Market services ex volatile 1.6%mth/4.0%yr. September Quarter CPI: Headline
1.3%qtr/3.9%yr; Trimmed Mean 1.0%qtr/3.7%yr.
The CPI rose 0.8% in the September month, stronger than Westpac’s 0.6% estimate and the

market’s expectation of 0.7%. The annual pace picked up from 4.0%yr to 4.3%yr, The seasonally
adjusted CPI rose 0.6% in September highlighting some positive seasonality in the month.

### Page 2

Key factors driving the headline monthly result were: automotive fuels (9.4%mth); domestic holiday
travel & accommodation (8.0%mth); international holiday travel & accommodation (7.2%mth); and
property rates & charges (5.5%mth). There were modest offsets from: women’s garments (–
3.1%mth); household textiles (–6.6%mth); and accessories (–4.1%mth).
The Trimmed Mean (TM) gained 0.3% in September, spot on both Westpac and market expectations
with the annual pace lifting from 3.5%yr (revised from 3.6%yr) to 3.6%yr. The six-month annualised

pace is now 4.0%yr the fastest in the short history of this series.
Headline CPI lifted 1.3% in the September quarter compared to an expectation of 1.3% by Westpac
and 1.2% by the market. This saw the annual pace hold at 3.9%yr.
The RBA’s key underlying inflation measure, the quarterly TM, lifted 1.0% vs an expectation of
0.9% by Westpac and market estimate of 1.0%. The annual pace lifted from 3.6%yr to 3.7%yr
with the six-month annualised pace accelerating to 3.7%yr from 3.4%yr.

Overall, the estimates come in broadly as expected so we see minimal risk to our current December
quarter forecast of 1.3%qtr for the headline CPI. Softer housing inflation does present a very modest
downside risk to our 0.9%qtr for the TM but there are offsetting gains elsewhere.
The September CPI update revealed underlying inflationary pressures continuing even while a
moderation in housing inflation (dwellings in particular) is unfolding slightly faster than we had

anticipated. As such, the near-term risk for inflation remains to the upside. The RBA will remain alert
to these near-term inflationary risks and as so, we hold to the view that the RBA will raise rates again
at the November meeting.

### Page 3

Month of September CPI
The monthly CPI rose 0.8%mth in September, above both our expectation of a 0.6%mth increase and
the market forecast of 0.7%mth. This lifted the annual pace by 0.3ppts to 4.3%yr. On a seasonally
adjusted basis, the CPI increased 0.6%mth.
The key contributors to the monthly increase were: automotive fuel (9.4%mth, +0.3ppts); and holiday
travel & accommodation (7.6%mth, +0.5ppts), alongside a smaller contribution from the annual reset in

property rates (5.5%mth, +0.1ppt). There was no single major offset. Instead, several components
across household furnishings and clothing & footwear recorded modest declines, together subtracting a
little over 0.1ppt from the monthly result.
The monthly trimmed mean (TM) rose 0.3%mth, in line with both our and the market’s expectations.
The annual pace edged up to 3.6%yr, although the August outcome was revised down to 3.5%yr.
Underlying inflation has remained broadly stuck in the 3.5–3.6%yr range since May. On a 3m/3m basis,

the monthly TM was 1.1%.

### Page 4

Market services excluding volatile items and holiday travel were flat in the month, with annual inflation
easing to 3.8%yr, its softest pace since November 2025. On a seasonally adjusted basis, monthly
inflation moderated across most components in this measure, with telecommunications services, rents
and other financial services the key exceptions. However, all but two components are still recording
annual price growth above 3%, suggesting that disinflation across market services remains slow and

relatively narrow. The persistence of these underlying price pressures is likely to remain a concern for
the RBA.

### Page 5

Housing inflation was notably softer in the month, rising 0.3%mth to be 5.2% higher over the year.
Rents increased 0.2%mth, slightly below our expectation for a 0.3%mth rise. The biannual indexation
of Commonwealth Rent Assistance occurred late in the month, weighing on the September outcome,
with the remainder of the impact expected to flow through in October. The ABS noted that rents would
have risen 0.3%mth in the absence of the measure, while on a seasonally adjusted basis rents

increased 0.4%mth.
New dwelling inflation was flat in the month, the softest reading since May 2025. Monthly inflation has
now slowed in each successive month since May 2026, suggesting homebuilders’ pricing power is
becoming increasingly constrained. The ABS noted that some project home builders raised base prices
this month only to be offset by some builders increasing discounts and promotional offers in response
to slowing demand. The annual pace eased to 4.7%yr. Dwelling repair costs were also a downside

surprise, falling 0.1%mth, their first decline since February 2025, although prices remain 3.4% higher
over the year. Some of this softness was offset by a firmer-than-expected increase in property rates
and charges, which rose 5.6%mth.
Recently, we have also focused on audio, visual and computing given the potential for spillovers from
strong global demand for semiconductors and other computing resources associated with the AI build
out. Prices for both equipment and services came in weaker than August, with equipment prices falling

1.1%mth, while services rose 0.3%mth. Despite this, annual price growth remains relatively high for
these categories, running at 4.6% and 4.9% respectively. Some of this pressure might also be showing

### Page 6

through in gaming, toys and hobbies, which is running at 5.2%mth/15.5%yr.
Overall, the breadth of inflation moderated for a second consecutive month. The share of the basket
running above 3%yr fell from 40% to 35%, meanwhile the share below 2%yr lifted from 27% to 32%.
Our replication of the San Francisco Fed’s inflation shock momentum served as a good guide to the
quarterly TM. With the full monthly reads in for the September quarter in, this measure does signal
some persistence/upside pressures for the quarterly TM going into the coming quarters. It is worth
cautioning that this measure will be impacted as the ABS refines its seasonal adjustment process for
the monthly indexes.

Compared to our expectations, the key surprises were:
Upside surprises
Downside surprises
Fruit & vegetables was the key upside surprise, 0.6%mth against our expectations for 3.7%mth fall.
Holiday travel & accommodation also surprised to the upside, following a run of downside surprises
since early this year. It rose 7.6%mth against our expectations for a 4.4%mth lift. 
Automotive fuel rose 9.4%mth against expectations of an 8.3%mth lift.

### Page 7

September Quarter CPI
Clothing & footwear was the key downside surprise with prices falling 2.7%mth against our
expectations for a 0.2%mth fall. The misses were broad-based: garments (–2.3%mth vs –2.0%mth);
footwear (–2.5%mth vs 1.8%mth); and accessories & clothing services (–4.1%mth vs 2.8%mth).
Household textiles declined 6.6%mth against our expectations for a 3.2%mth fall. Furniture &
furnishings rose by less-than-expected 0.4%mth vs 1.6%mth. Household appliances, utensils and

tools also came in softer, –3.2%mth vs –2.4%mth.
Dairy prices fell 1.2%mth against our expectations for a 0.1%mth lift.

### Page 8

The September quarter CPI printed spot on our expectation of 1.3%. The key factor driving this
increase was the slightly stronger surge in auto fuel (7.8%, +0.27ppt) and holiday travel &
accommodation (2.7%qtr, +0.16ppt) being offset by a slightly softer than expected increase in housing
(1.1%qtr, +0.24ppt) with rents and dwellings both increasing 1.0% in the quarter and utilities lifting
1.2%. Food was also slightly stronger than expected (0.7%qtr, +0.12ppt) and while not significant to the
overall result it is interesting to track the ongoing rise in motor vehicle prices (0.3%qtr) despite a very
competitive market with Chinese manufacturers rapidly gaining market share over legacy brands.

It is worth noting that food was stronger than we expected (0.7% vs 0.5%) with a robust 1.7% increase
in restaurant meals and a solid 1.2% increase in take away & fast foods outweighing the –0.4% decline
in fresh fruit & vegetables and meat & seafoods. Overall, market services ex volatile prices increased a
very solid 1.2%qtr but with the annual pace easing to 3.2%yr from 3.36%yr.
Outside of housing the other main downside surprise in the quarter was the –0.6% fall in clothing &
footwear (+0.3% expected) with prices falling across just about all categories of garments, footwear
and accessories.
September Quarter Trimmed Mean

### Page 9

Recall that the TM is calculated from seasonally adjust consumption expenditure classes so any
numbers quoted here may not be the same as the original estimates quoted from the CPI. The TM gain
of 1.0% in the quarter was slightly stronger than we had expected even though dwellings (which have a
weight of almost 11% in the TM) was a touch softer at 1.0% compared to the 1.1% we had pencilled in.
Rents (1.0%, 10% weight) also made a solid contribution as did restaurant meals (1.7%qr, 6% weight),

take away & fast food (1.2%, 4% weight), other financial service (1.0%, 6% weight). Domestic travel
was in the TM (1.0%, 4% weight) as was medical & hospital service (1.0%, weight 5%). The range of
the TM was from 0.0% (motor vehicles) to 1.7% (beer).
What does this mean for our December quarter forecast and into 2027?
We are working though the monthly results to see what changes they may imply for individual
component inflationary momentum. Our preliminary analysis suggests that the softer trend in dwellings

is not enough, on its own, to change our Q4 CPI forecast of 1.3%. It does, however, suggest that if all
else held equal (which is not the case as there is likely to be some offsetting stronger gains elsewhere)
the moderation in dwelling inflation presents a very modest downside risk our Q4 TM estimate of 0.9%.
We are still working the numbers and will publish an updated inflation profile as soon as we can. In
addition, the overall momentum in underlying inflationary pressures suggest the downside risk to the

TM from dwellings is more likely to appear in 2027 than in the last quarter of 2026.

CENTRAL BANK MEETINGS

Bank of Thailand (BOT) (October 28)

We expect the BOT to maintain its policy 
rate at 1.00% at its 28 October meeting, 
extending its pause for a fourth 
consecutive decision as it balances 
inflationary pressures against uneven 
and subdued economic growth. Our view 
is aligned with fixed market income 
pricing. Headline inflation rose anew to 
2.8% yoy in September 2026, driven by 
higher energy, food, and core price 
increases, but remained within the upper 
end of the central bank’s 1 -3% target 
range. As the BOT is still likely to look 
through what it views as temporary, 
supply-driven inflationary pressu res, it 
will likely refrain from tightening policy, 
and keep interest rates accommodative 
to support the soft and uneven growth 
cycle.

European Central Bank (October 29)

We expect the ECB to extend its pause, 
keeping the deposit facility rate at 2.5%. 
Market participants will instead focus on 
the central bank’s assessment of rising 
French and Italian bond yields and 
widening spreads versus Bunds. While 
we do not expect dir ect references to 
individual countries, the ECB is likely to 
signal that recent developments in 
France reflect the country’s weaker 
fiscal and debt profile rather than a 
broader systemic concern. Policymakers 
will seek to differentiate between a 
localised bond sell -off and a Eurozone -
wide market disruption, justifying their 
decision to monitor developments rather 
than implement specific measures at this 
juncture. Meanwhile, inflation continues 
to rise, with little relief expected in 
upcoming readings given elevated 
global fuel prices. 
Bank of Japan (October 30)

The Bank of Japan is expected to keep 
the overnight call rate unchanged at 1.25% 
at this meeting, pausing after the 25bp 
hike in September. The BOJ is likely to 
assess the impact of the previous rate 
hike before taking further action. The 
recent stabilisat ion of the yen also 
reduces the urgency of another hike at 
this meeting.

The tightening bias should remain intact. 
Growth and inflation remain broadly on 
track, leaving room for the BOJ to revise 
up its FY2026 –FY2027 GDP and CPI 
forecasts. Wage growth has also 
remained strong, with Rengo reportedly 
targeting wage increases of 6% in 2027, 
reinforcing the BOJ’s confidence that 
underlying inflation can be sustained 
around its 2% target. We continue to 
expect the policy rate to rise to 1.50% in 
December 2026 or January 2027, 
followed by a further increase to 1.75% 
by 2Q 2027.

FORTHCOMING DATA 
RELEASES

Hong Kong SAR

Export growth is expected to accelerate 
from 53.0% yoy in August to 55.0% in 
September, in line with the pickup in 
China’s export growth from 25.0% to 
36.3%. As a key re-export hub for China,

### Page 3

The Week Ahead October 23, 2026 
                                                                                                                                         3

Hong Kong should benefit from robust 
AI-related electronics trade, with 
electrical machinery, equipment and 
parts exports to Hong Kong accounting 
for 20.6% of China’s total exports. 
Import growth is also expected to remain 
firm, supported by resilient expo rt 
orders and gradually improving 
investment sentiment, sustaining 
demand for intermediate and capital 
goods.

Singapore

Singapore’s industrial production (IP) 
likely remained in expansion for a 13th 
consecutive month in September 2026, 
although we expect growth to moderate 
to 7.6% yoy from August’s strong 15.4% 
yoy pace. IP growth continued to be 
underpinned by the electronics and 
precision engineering clusters, which 
has been key drivers of Singapore’s 
manufacturing strength, reflecting 
sustained demand for artificial 
intelligence-related hardware. However, 
performance across clusters remained 
uneven, with chemicals and biomedical 
manufacturing likely contracting, and 
tempering overall gains.

South Korea

The preliminary estimate for 3Q26 GDP 
is likely to show growth remaining solid 
at 3.2% yoy , slightly below 3.7% in 2Q 
and 3.8% in 1Q. Nominal GDP growth is 
likely to remain in double digits, 
significantly outpacing real GDP growth, 
thanks to improved terms of trade amid 
rising memory export prices. By 
expenditure, exports picked up in 3Q in 
both nominal and real terms, supported 
by stronger semiconductor shipments. 
Investment also appeared robust, as 
reflected in the equipment investment 
estimation index and machinery order 
index. Private consumption, however, 
appeared to weaken amid the sharp 
KOSPI correction since July following  
the introduction of leveraged ETF 
regulations. Our full -year GDP growth 
forecasts of 3.5% for 2026 and 2.4% for 
2027 remain on track.

Taiwan

The preliminary estimate for 3Q GDP is 
likely to show growth remaining strong 
at 12 –13% yoy , close to 12.9% in 2Q, 
albeit below the peak of 15.4% in 1Q. 
Exports maintained strong double -digit 
growth in 3Q (44.6% yoy  in nominal 
terms and 21.1% in real terms), supported 
by robust shipments of semiconductors, 
servers and other AI -related products. 
Machinery investment also sustained 
strong double-digit growth, underpinned 
by higher capacity utilisation in the ICT 
sector and a resulting increase in capital 
goods imports. Private consumption 
appeared to have strengthened further, 
supported by stock market gains, 
improving wage growth, and the 
associated wealth and income effects. 
Construction investment remained the 
weak s pot, weighed down by elevated 
inventories in the new-home market and 
the ongoing need for inventory digestion. 
We maintain our 2026 and 2027 GDP 
growth forecasts at 11.6% and 5.6%, 
respectively, with risks tilted to the 
upside.

United States: Elevated inflation and high growth
02/10/2026 4
August 2026: Marked improvement in services
business confidence, from an already high level
September 2026: Manufacturing business

confidence remains stable at a high level
August 2026: Increasing inflationary pressures September 2026: Supplier deliveries do not get

August 2026: Consumers are a bit more
concerned about their personal finances
August 2026: Headline inflation resists to the
upside
U.S. macroeconomic performance has remained solid since the onset of the shock, as illustrated in particular by Q2 growth
of +3.8% and +9% (annualized quarterly rate) in consumer spending and private nonresidential investment, respectively.

In the manufacturing sector, business sentiment has gained momentum despite the shock, while the impact of this shock
on the services sector has remained very limited. The immediate reaction of consumer confidence was, however, more
negative before beginning to improve, which was nevertheless interrupted in August (latest available data for households’
sentiment on their future personal financial situation). The University of Michigan’s composite indicator continued to

deteriorate in September, due to the sharp rise in fuel prices. But declining confidence still does not translate into reduced
spending—at least not at the aggregate level.
On the inflation front, the spike immediately following the outbreak of the conflict in Iran was more pronounced than in
the months following Russia’s invasion of Ukraine. The decline in June still left inflation at a high level, but its stability in
July and August was notable (3.4% y/y), as was the latest—the third consecutive—slight decline in inflation excluding

energy in August (2.5% y/y, same rate as in February). Given the existing inflationary pressures, however, inflation is highly
likely to resume its upward trend in September.
Source: BLS, ISM, NFIB, University of Michigan, BNP Paribas

重點更新 
3Q26 AI 電源占比持續提升 
3Q26 營收 574.03 億元（+8.9% QoQ、+27.9% YoY） ，低於原估3.1%，受惠 AI
伺服器電源及 Cloud 業務持續成長，其中 Cloud/AIoT 營收 337.53 億元（+57% 
YoY） ，占整體營收59%，惟 PC 及消費性電子需求相對疲弱。毛利 148.67 億元
（+3.6% QoQ） ，毛利率25.9%（-1.3ppts QoQ） ，高於原估1.9ppts，主要受惠
高附加價值 AI 電源比重提升及營運效率改善 ； 營業費用及其他營業損益淨額62.11
億元（+1.1% QoQ） ，占營收10.8%，營業利益 86.55 億元（+5.5% QoQ） ，營益
率 15.1%（-0.5ppts QoQ） ，高於原估3.1ppts。業外收益 11.42 億元，稅後淨利
74.29 億元，EPS 3.27 元創單季新高。整體而言，光寶在機種的晶片短缺供應影響
較小，支撐獲利持續成長。

2027 年 800VDC 放量部分遞延壓抑獲利 
預估 2027 年營收 2,529.25 億元（+19.8% YoY） ，較原估下修5.0%，成長主要來
自 CSP ASIC 平台 8.5kW PSU/BBU、NVIDIA Vera Rubin 18.3kW PSU與 110kW 
Power Shelf 持續出貨，以及新客戶與低軌衛星電源需求增加。惟記憶體缺料、客
戶資料中心建置遞延，加上 800VDC Power Rack 仍處於送樣驗證及初期導入階
段，較大規模貢獻可能延至 2028 年。預估毛利率 25.4%（+0.1ppts YoY） ，營業
費用 334.91 億元（+34.7% YoY） ，營業利益307.86 億元（+7.6% YoY） ，歸母稅
後淨利 266.08 億元 ，EPS 11.49 元 。 光寶已由單一PSU 延伸至 BBU、Power Shelf
及 Power Rack 系統整合，但高毛利新產品放量時程仍須觀察。

### Page 2

報告內容 
3Q26 AI 電源與 Cloud 需求推升獲利創高 
3Q26 營收 574.03 億元（+8.9% QoQ、+27.9% YoY） ，低於原估3.1%，受惠 AI 伺服器電源及 Cloud 業務持續成
長，其中 Cloud/AIoT 營收 337.53 億元（+57% YoY） ，占整體營收59%，惟 PC 及消費性電子需求相對疲弱。毛
利 148.67 億元（+3.6% QoQ） ，毛利率25.9%（-1.3ppts QoQ） ，高於原估1.9ppts，主要受惠高附加價值 AI 電
源比重提升及營運效率改善；營業費用及其他營業損益淨額 62.11 億元（+1.1% QoQ） ，占營收10.8%，營業利益
86.55 億元（+5.5% QoQ） ，營益率15.1%（-0.5ppts QoQ） ，高於原估3.1ppts。業外收益 11.42 億元，稅後淨
利 74.29 億元 ，EPS 3.27 元創單季新高 。整體而言 ， 光寶在既有機種的晶片短缺供應影響較小 ， 支撐獲利持續成長 。
